## 2026-07-20 — Conditional/comma wrappers around captured field offsets

- No external documentation was needed. Existing C conditional and comma semantics imply that only the selected conditional branch executes and the comma left operand executes before the selected field pointer; the warning-free fixture keeps each marker in a separate full expression.
- Wrapping a captured named, anonymous, or union-containing array field before `+ 1`, reverse `1 +`, or `&field[1]` must preserve the chosen containing root/path and absolute field index through C parameter adjustment. Cust's existing pointer metadata already composes correctly across these wrappers.
- Seventy-two generated alias cases and 13 exact diagnostics pass without production changes. The generated RED was a harness oracle bug: comma routes incremented the comma marker but not the selected-route marker expected by the shared check. After correcting the generator, the model passed. The warning-free fixture `adjusted_aggregate_parameter_wrapped_compound_literal_field_offset_routes.c` returns 44 under Cust, GCC, and Clang. See `references/cust-wrapped-captured-field-offset-adjusted-parameters.md`.

## 2026-07-20 — Nonzero offsets on captured compound-literal array fields

- Aggregate compound literals captured once by address retain stable hidden containing storage. Applying `field + 1`, reverse `1 + field`, or `&field[1]` must advance the selected embedded aggregate-array field pointer without rebasing its owner/path metadata when C array-parameter adjustment copies the pointer slot.
- A callee based at field element one can legally use `items[-1]` to reach the preceding element. Exact Cust upper/lower diagnostics report absolute field indexes 3 and -1 for a length-three embedded field; subtraction across named versus anonymous captured paths remains interpreter-only because native C unrelated-array subtraction is undefined.
- Fifty-four generated alias cases and 13 exact diagnostics pass without production changes. The warning-free fixture `adjusted_aggregate_parameter_compound_literal_field_offset_routes.c` returns 34 under Cust, GCC, and Clang.

## 2026-07-20 — Nonzero offsets on wrapped direct literals through adjusted parameters

- Array compound literals decay before `+ 1`, reverse `1 + literal`, or `&literal[1]`; passing that pointer to an adjusted aggregate parameter must retain the hidden root's original length and current outer index rather than rebasing storage at zero.
- A callee receiving element one may legally reach the preceding same-array element with `items[-1]`. Cust, GCC, and Clang agree on the warning-free fixture at exit 37; exact Cust diagnostics report absolute root indexes 3 and -1 for upper/lower failures from a length-three root.
- Fifty-four generated alias cases, nine const-typedef wrapper combinations, and 13 exact diagnostics pass. See `tests/fixtures/compat/valid/adjusted_aggregate_parameter_wrapped_direct_compound_literal_offset_routes.c`; no production change was needed because existing hidden-root pointer metadata already preserved the nonzero base.

## 2026-07-20 — Conditional/comma-wrapped direct literals through adjusted parameters

- Conditional array operands decay to the selected pointer before adjusted-parameter binding; only the selected compound-literal initializer executes. A comma wrapper evaluates its scalar left operand before the literal on the right. Cust's existing pointer classifiers and hidden literal roots already compose correctly across both forms.
- Distinct function arguments remain unsequenced, so use separate marker objects for each argument in native fixtures. Cross-root subtraction stays interpreter-only because unrelated-array subtraction is undefined in C.
- Thirty-six generated alias cases, six const-typedef wrapper cases, and 11 exact diagnostics pass. The warning-free fixture returns 69 under Cust/GCC/Clang. See `references/cust-adjusted-aggregate-parameter-wrapped-direct-compound-literals.md`.

## 2026-07-20 — Direct aggregate-array literals through adjusted parameters

- A direct `(struct Item[N]){...}` argument decays once while binding an adjusted aggregate parameter; embedded pointers derived in the callee must retain the hidden literal root plus outer and inner indexes through forwarding and copied pointer slots.
- Use separate marker objects for separate direct literal arguments. Clang `-Werror` correctly rejects two unsequenced `++marker` modifications in one call, even when a final count would look deterministic; independent `left_marker`/`right_marker` objects preserve a warning-free defined oracle.
- Existing Cust behavior passed immediately. Forty-eight generated cases plus 11 exact diagnostics pass, and the warning-free fixture returns 72 under Cust/GCC/Clang. See `references/cust-adjusted-aggregate-parameter-direct-compound-literal-arguments.md`.

## 2026-07-19 — Compound-literal outer arrays through adjusted parameters

- Capturing an aggregate compound literal's address once gives its named, anonymous, or union-containing outer-array fields a stable hidden root for the enclosing block. C array adjustment copies only the selected outer pointer slot; it must retain literal root, containing path, outer index, embedded field, and inner index through helper calls.
- A fixed matrix can model same-element, same-array-distinct, cross-field, and separate-literal relationships while six initializer markers prove roots are not re-evaluated. Cross-root subtraction remains interpreter-only because native C leaves unrelated-array subtraction undefined.
- Existing Cust behavior passed immediately. The warning-free fixture returns 67 under Cust/GCC/Clang; details and recovery commands are in `references/cust-adjusted-aggregate-parameter-compound-literal-outer-arrays.md`.

## 2026-07-19 — Nested containing paths through adjusted aggregate parameters

- C array adjustment preserves the concrete outer array selected from root storage, a direct aggregate field, a nested named or anonymous field, or a union member. Model that outer owner/path separately from the selected outer and inner indexes; helper calls and copied parameter slots do not create new storage roots.
- Coverage counters are part of the generator contract. Expanding storage variants while retaining the old route formula initially left anonymous/union path counts below the required floor; use a dedicated nested-storage selector for the alias model rather than perturbing the earlier identity model's root/field balance.
- Same-root equality/mutation is a valid native oracle, while subtraction/order across different containing paths or separate roots stays interpreter-only. The warning-free fixture returns 32 under Cust/GCC/Clang. See `references/cust-adjusted-aggregate-parameter-nested-path-alias-mutations.md`.

## 2026-07-19 — Ordered alias mutation through adjusted aggregate parameters

- C array adjustment copies the outer aggregate pointer slot; embedded pointers captured from it retain the concrete outer owner/path/index. Model two mutable writers and a `const` reader by storage identity, apply writes in source order, and separately score intermediate/final reader observations plus caller pointer identities.
- An outer aggregate-array field element selecting a nested array is represented by `Expr::StructFieldArrayElementGet`. Pointer-value classification must include `StructFieldType::Array` and `StructFieldType::StructArray`, while assignment-result variants remain pointer-valued only for ordinary pointer fields. Runtime pointer evaluation must try embedded-array base decay before pointer-field reads.
- The warning-free compiler-oracle fixture uses only defined same-element aliases and ABI-independent value/identity checks. Cust, GCC, and Clang return 40 with `-std=c11 -Wall -Wextra -Werror`. Implementation and recovery details are in `references/cust-adjusted-aggregate-parameter-alias-mutations.md`.

## 2026-07-19 — Model-based recursive identity through adjusted aggregate parameters

- Model adjusted parameters by the argument's concrete storage identity plus outer aggregate index, nested field path, and inner element index. C array adjustment copies a pointer slot; it does not flatten field-decayed outer-array identity or merge distinct embedded arrays from different outer elements.
- Balance direct `&items[i].nested.field[j]` and reverse `&j[items[i].nested.field]` spellings, and initialize generated cells through the captured pointers. Initializing via an unrelated direct nested scalar write on `wrapper.items[i]` exercises a different AST seam and can obscure the adjusted-parameter property under test.
- Native C safely oracles same-embedded-array equality/difference/ordering and equality across distinct roots, but cross-root subtraction/ordering remains interpreter-only because those operations are undefined in C. The warning-free static fixture returns 35 under Cust/GCC/Clang; the generated matrix keeps cross-root diagnostic assertions under panic guards.

## 2026-07-19 — Embedded array fields through adjusted aggregate parameters

- Specialized `StructElementArray*` syntax does not reveal whether the outer aggregate array is stored directly or arrived through C array adjustment. Resolve `items[i]` as an interpreter-owned aggregate pointer first, then inspect the selected field metadata to distinguish embedded arrays from pointer-valued fields; this keeps outer and inner expressions single-evaluation.
- When an adjusted parameter receives an outer array decayed from an aggregate field (`read(wrapper.items)`), a second embedded aggregate-array index cannot be represented by the flat root-array pointer variants. `PointerValue::NestedStructArrayElement` retains the parent pointer, nested field path, and selected index and delegates immutable/mutable aggregate resolution recursively.
- Every new pointer target needs explicit identity handling. The adjacent equality RED produced 5 instead of 6 because `pointer_eq()` omitted the recursive target even though pointer difference and ordering worked. Compare parent pointers with `pointer_eq()` rather than derived `PartialEq`; this preserves interpreter-owned storage identity and avoids structural comparison of reference-counted storage. The warning-free fixture returns 82 under Cust/GCC/Clang.

## 2026-07-19 — Pointer-field slot updates through adjusted aggregate-array parameters

- `StructElementSet` / `StructElementCompoundSet` and pointer increment over `StructElementGet` keep their specialized AST when the containing array parameter has adjusted to `Value::Pointer`. Their shared mutating resolver must therefore select `indexed_struct_pointer()` for adjusted parameters and retain `find_struct_element_pointer()` only for stored aggregate arrays.
- A const array-parameter pointer slot (`T items[const N]`) prevents rebinding the parameter but does not qualify the aggregate pointee. Do not run ordinary variable-slot mutability checks before mutation through an adjusted parameter; instead reject only `pointer_variable_points_to_const(name)` and let aggregate field assignment enforce a const pointer field slot separately.
- Clang `-Wall -Wextra -Werror` rejects side-effecting pointer assignment/increment operands inside `sizeof` under `-Wunevaluated-expression`; keep marker-based non-evaluation coverage interpreter-only and use side-effect-free pointer-size relationships in `c_compat`. The compiler-neutral fixture returns 63 under Cust/GCC/Clang. See `references/cust-adjusted-aggregate-array-parameter-pointer-field-updates.md`.

## 2026-07-19 — Nested-holder pointer-field subscripts through adjusted aggregate-array parameters

- A bare `choices[i].nested.pointer_field[j]` keeps the specialized `StructElementArrayGet`/set/update/address AST even inside a function where `union Choice choices[]` has undergone C array-to-pointer adjustment. Runtime helpers therefore cannot infer storage shape from syntax: direct arrays are `Value::StructArray`, adjusted parameters are `Value::Pointer { ty: PointeeType::Struct(..) }`.
- Resolve the containing element with `indexed_struct_pointer()` first and fall back to stored-array `find_struct_element_pointer()`. Reuse that shared resolver for reads, writes, compound assignment, increment, and address-of so each outer index is evaluated once and both named struct/union pointer targets retain interpreter-owned identity.
- `struct_element_field_metadata()`, pointer-result type/const inference, truthiness classification, aggregate assignment-result typing, and non-evaluating `sizeof` should derive selected field metadata from the declared aggregate type for either storage form. Do not inspect/evaluate a first runtime element for adjusted parameters. Warning-free GCC/Clang and Cust return 32 on the nested named/anonymous/union fixture. See `references/cust-adjusted-aggregate-array-parameter-pointer-fields.md`.

## 2026-07-19 — Aggregate-valued named aggregate-array pointer-field consumers

- `eval_struct_expr()` and `aggregate_expr_type_name()` already recognized aggregate-valued `StructElementArrayGet`/`Set`, but function binding still used a second hand-maintained allowlist in `eval_struct_argument()`. Add both variants there so direct indexed pointer-field reads and copy-assignment results bind by value while preserving established aggregate type diagnostics.
- Parenthesized aggregate assignment results followed by `.` re-enter `parse_postfix_suffix()`. `StructElementArraySet` must join the aggregate-valued postfix allowlist so `(nodes[i].points[j] = replacement).field` lowers to `AggregateFieldGet`; runtime evaluation and non-evaluating `sizeof` then reuse existing assignment-result aggregate metadata and deep-copy paths.
- Warning-free `gcc` and `clang -std=c11 -Wall -Wextra -Werror` executions return 201 like Cust. Keep one-time evaluation markers in separate statements so native operand-order differences do not weaken the oracle.

## 2026-07-18 — Ordinary named aggregate-array pointer-field subscripts

- `nodes[i].field[j]` is represented by `StructElementArrayGet` (and matching set/compound variants) for both embedded arrays and stored pointer fields. Runtime and metadata dispatch must inspect `StructFieldType` before choosing the legacy embedded-array path; scalar pointees use checked scalar pointer indexing, while named aggregate pointees use aggregate pointer offset/field resolution.
- Supporting aggregate pointees requires more than postfix `.field` routing: `aggregate_expr_type_name()` and `eval_struct_expr()` must recognize aggregate-valued `StructElementArrayGet`/`Set` results. Reads deep-clone the selected aggregate, while copy assignment should reuse the established aggregate-pointer assignment path so const/type checks and assignment-result copies stay consistent.
- Metadata-only `sizeof` must not clone/evaluate a runtime pointer. Resolve the pointer expression's `PointeeType::Struct` and selected field type from declarations. Warning-free `cc -std=c11 -Wall -Wextra -Werror` coverage returns 80 and matches Cust; side-effecting one-time-evaluation checks remain interpreter-focused to avoid relying on unspecified C operand order.

## 2026-07-18 — Ordinary named aggregate-array pointer-field dispatch

- `nodes[i].field` uses `StructElementGet`/`Set`/`CompoundSet`, distinct from both direct aggregate fields and embedded aggregate-array element fields. Pointer classification must consult aggregate field metadata before the legacy embedded-array decay fallback; mutation must resolve the element through a const-root-aware helper while reads remain legal through const aggregate slots.
- Pointer-valued assignment, compound-assignment, and increment results must participate in `expr_is_pointer_value()`, `pointer_expr_pointee_type()`, and `pointer_expr_points_to_const()` before evaluation. For assignment results, merging destination pointee constness with RHS constness preserves the established one-level conversion boundary.
- Warning-free `cc -std=c11 -Wall -Wextra -Werror` coverage returns 17 and matches Cust. A follow-up probe (`nodes[0].cursor[1]`) isolates the next distinct AST seam: it reports `struct field 'cursor' is not an array`, so pointer-field subscript/address routing remains separate backlog work.

## Findings

- 2026-07-18: Pointer-valued final fields selected by `StructFieldArrayElementGet`/`Set`/`CompoundSet` need the same three metadata surfaces as other pointer expressions: `pointer_expr_pointee_type`, `pointer_expr_points_to_const`, and `expr_is_pointer_value`. Runtime evaluation should resolve the containing aggregate element once and delegate final-field reads/writes to established struct-pointer helpers. Reads must not reuse the mutating resolver: doing so rejects legal reads through const containing aggregates. Assignment/compound/increment routes retain the mutating resolver for root/field/slot const checks and concrete pointee validation. `Expr::Increment` result constness should delegate to its target expression rather than special-case only pointer variables. Native C confirms direct/nested/reverse replacement and updates with a warning-free exit-9 fixture; side-effecting `sizeof` remains interpreter-only. A minimized adjacent probe shows ordinary `StructElementGet` pointer fields (`nodes[i].cursor`) remain a separate follow-up.

- 2026-07-18: `StructFieldArrayElementSet` / `StructFieldArrayElementCompoundSet` must capture the selected aggregate element once and then route final-field reads/writes through struct-pointer helpers. The old direct compound path evaluated a side-effecting index during both read and write, while reverse forms treated the pointer operand as a scalar. A shared `struct_field_array_element_assignment_pointer()` now centralizes direct/reverse resolution and const checks and is reused by increment/decrement. Replacement retains this AST path's established RHS-before-target order; compound assignment captures/reads the target, evaluates RHS once, then writes through the captured pointer. Because the same AST also indexes aggregate pointer fields, resolve those before root/field const checks: `const T *field` protects the pointee, while `T * const field` and pointer slots inside const containing aggregates still permit element mutation. Cust/GCC/Clang return 16 on the warning-free fixture; side-effecting `sizeof` remains interpreter-only under Clang's `-Wunevaluated-expression` boundary. An adjacent pointer-valued selected-field probe (`box.nodes[0].cursor = values + 1`) reports `pointer value used as scalar` and is the next slice. See `references/cust-embedded-aggregate-array-element-field-assignment.md`.

- 2026-07-18: `StructFieldArrayElementGet` increment/decrement must resolve the selected aggregate element to one interpreter-owned pointer before reading and writing its scalar field. Calling the existing read and assignment helpers independently evaluates a side-effecting direct index twice; the captured-pointer route supports both direct embedded arrays and scalar-field reverse aggregate subscripts while preserving prefix/postfix results. Direct routes must enforce root/aggregate-field constness before pointer resolution, and directly named const aggregate arrays on reverse routes need an explicit variable-mutability check because direct-array read-only checks normally happen in assignment helpers. Runtime-defined direct/reverse forms compile warning-free and return 14 under GCC and Clang; side-effecting `sizeof(++lvalue)` remains interpreter-only because Clang promotes `-Wunevaluated-expression`. The adjacent `selector.value[points].value += 2` probe still reports `struct variable 'points' used as scalar`, and direct compound assignment uses separate read/write index evaluation; fix both next. See `references/cust-embedded-aggregate-array-element-field-increment.md`.

- 2026-07-18: Assignment and compound-assignment expressions retain the type of their lvalue in unevaluated `sizeof`, so Cust's reverse-subscript result variants must preserve the same metadata dispatch as their corresponding get variants. `ArraySet`/`ArrayCompoundSet`, `StructArraySet`/`StructArrayCompoundSet`, `StructElementSet`/`StructElementCompoundSet`, and `StructFieldArrayElementSet`/`StructFieldArrayElementCompoundSet` now inspect the retained index operand and reuse scalar-variable/scalar-field reverse-subscript pointee inference before direct-index fallback; `Expr::Increment` already delegates to its target's non-evaluating metadata. Local GCC accepts the side-effecting fixture under `-std=c11 -Wall -Wextra -Werror` and returns 13 like Cust. Local Clang rejects every assignment/compound/increment operand with `-Werror,-Wunevaluated-expression`, so the fixture stays interpreter-focused and is not registered in compiler-neutral `c_compat`; weakening global oracle flags would hide unrelated warnings. The probe also exposed a separate parser/runtime gap for incrementing `StructFieldArrayElementGet` lvalues, recorded as the next backlog item.

- 2026-07-18: Non-evaluating `sizeof` must resolve Cust's specialized reverse-subscript AST from operand shape metadata, not from the syntactic left name. For `ArrayGet`, `StructArrayGet`, `StructElementGet`, and `StructFieldArrayElementGet`, first prove the left variable/field is scalar, require `expr_is_pointer_value(pointer_operand)`, then obtain its scalar/aggregate pointee through `pointer_expr_pointee_type()` and size that type or selected aggregate field. This avoids runtime index/pointer evaluation and preserves the runtime shape diagnostics. The audit also found direct `sizeof(aggregate_pointer[index].field)` needed `sizeof_struct_element_field()` to accept named aggregate pointer metadata rather than only aggregate arrays. GCC and Clang warning-free C11 fixtures return 13 like Cust. Clang emits `-Wunevaluated-expression` for explicit side effects nested in `sizeof` under the repository's `-Werror`, so side-effect suppression stays in the Cust-only fixture while the native oracle uses thirteen ABI-independent direct/reverse type relationships.

- 2026-07-18: C11 subscript equivalence requires runtime result-shape handling when Cust's specialized parser sees a bare variable or aggregate field on the left of `[`: syntax alone cannot distinguish `array[index]` from `index[array]`. Preserve specialized direct-index AST nodes and resolve only scalar-left cases after runtime lookup; reuse interpreter-owned pointer offset/dereference targets so writes, address-of, concrete aggregate identity, const qualification, and bounds diagnostics stay aligned. Direct GCC and Clang `-std=c11 -Wall -Wextra -Werror` builds return 220 like Cust. An adjacent metadata-only probe shows `sizeof(index[values])` and `sizeof(index[points])` still fail with `variable 'index' is not an array`; fix this separately without evaluating either operand.

- 2026-07-18: Local GCC and Clang C11 probes reject pointer, aggregate, and void `_Static_assert` conditions as non-integer/non-integral constant expressions. Cust intentionally retains its existing runtime-evaluated scalar-condition subset rather than implementing full compile-time constant-expression enforcement, but `Stmt::StaticAssert` must use `eval()` rather than pointer-capable `eval_truthy()` so result shapes match the integer boundary. C subscripts consume full expressions: routing `parse_index_expr()` through assignment precedence enables conditional and assignment indexes, while a conservative numeric-non-lvalue check preserves the established `values[0 = 3;` missing-`]` diagnostic. The fixed matrix returns 97 under Cust/GCC/Clang. Web search was unavailable because Firecrawl was not configured; findings were verified with local `gcc -std=c11 -Wall -Wextra -Werror` and `clang` probes.

- 2026-07-17: Initializer elements already dispatch from declared destination metadata at runtime: `make_array_value()` and scalar/embedded-array field initialization use `eval()`, pointer fields enforce const and concrete pointee type around `eval_pointer()`, and nested aggregate fields use `eval_struct_expr()` with deep-copy/type checks. Positional entries are parser-resolved to destination fields while runtime loops retain source order, including backward designator indexes. Therefore 122 valid cases plus 17 exact mismatch cases passed as deliberate property/conformance closure without production changes. Keep native fixtures to at most one side-effecting expression per initializer declaration because C does not provide a useful cross-expression ordering oracle; GCC/Clang/Cust return 255. See `references/cust-initializer-element-context-classifier-modeling.md`.

- 2026-07-17: Declaration initializers and later assignment RHS values already dispatch from the destination's runtime kind: scalar declarations/assignments use `eval()`, pointers enforce const/type metadata around `eval_pointer()`, and aggregate declarations/assignments use `eval_struct_expr()` with deep-copy/type checks. Unlike the prior function-argument seam, no second partial aggregate AST allowlist exists here, so 58 valid route-context cases and 24 mismatch cases passed immediately as deliberate property/conformance closure. Keep native coverage to one side-effecting expression per statement; GCC/Clang/Cust return 180. See `references/cust-initializer-assignment-context-classifier-modeling.md`.

- 2026-07-17: Function parameter kind already selects the correct scalar, pointer, or aggregate evaluator, but aggregate argument binding had a second hand-maintained AST allowlist in `eval_struct_argument()`. It omitted every aggregate-valued assignment-result route even though `eval_struct_expr()` supported them, so `(target = replacement)`, `*slot = replacement`, direct/arrow aggregate-field assignments, root aggregate-array element assignments, and embedded aggregate-array element assignments all leaked `function '<name>' struct parameter '<param>' requires a struct argument`. Route `Assign`, `DerefSet`, `StructSet`, `StructPtrSet`, `ArraySet`, and `StructArraySet` through the established aggregate evaluator to preserve deep-copy, type, and const behavior. For native coverage, keep each call to one argument and store its result before reading side-effect counters so C's unspecified argument/operand evaluation order cannot affect the oracle. GCC/Clang/Cust return 173.

- 2026-07-17: Return expressions must preserve declared-type evaluation for non-void functions because those evaluator contexts define established pointer/aggregate/scalar diagnostics, but value-bearing returns in void functions need discard-context evaluation before function-level validation. The prior scalar path made `return pointer_expr;`, `return aggregate_expr;`, and `return void_call();` leak scalar-use errors instead of `void function '<name>' returned a value`. Void return expressions now evaluate exactly once via `eval_discard()`, while non-void behavior remains unchanged. Keep side-effecting compiler-oracle calls in temporaries before reading counters to avoid operand evaluation-order ambiguity. GCC/Clang/Cust return 199. See `references/cust-return-context-classifier-modeling.md`.

- 2026-07-17: Discard contexts must classify the complete expression result before runtime evaluation. The prior `eval_discard()` used scalar-evaluator failure text as pointer metadata, so pointer-valued commas executed their left operand twice; aggregate dereference copy assignment similarly probed its pointer with `eval_pointer(...).is_ok()` before evaluating it again. Metadata-first void/pointer/aggregate classification now selects one evaluator, accepts ordinary aggregate values in discard contexts, and evaluates void-valued comma/conditional expressions recursively in source order. GCC/Clang/Cust return 240 on the warning-free fixture. See `references/cust-discard-context-classifier-modeling.md`.

- 2026-07-17: Truthiness must classify the complete expression with `expr_is_pointer_value()` before runtime evaluation. The prior `eval_truthy()` match duplicated a partial pointer-valued AST list; arrow pointer-field assignment results such as `view->primary = (int *)0` fell through to scalar `eval()` and leaked `pointer value used as scalar` in loop/logical conditions. Pointer-valued truthiness now uses one `eval_pointer()` call plus `pointer_truthy()`, while scalar, aggregate, and void routes retain their contextual evaluators and diagnostics. Balanced null-route generation also showed that pointer casts delegated blindly to pointer evaluation: `(int *)(int)0` failed even though the scalar cast is an integer constant zero. Pointer casts now classify their operand, evaluate scalar operands once, accept zero as null, and retain `expected pointer expression` for nonzero values. GCC `-Werror=address` rejects obviously non-null array-address conditions, so native fixtures should route known pointers through a helper selected by runtime data. GCC/Clang/Cust return 227. See `references/cust-truthiness-classifier-modeling.md`.

- 2026-07-17: Relational ordering must classify both operand result shapes with `expr_is_pointer_value()` before evaluation. The prior branch called `eval_pointer()` for both operands whenever either was pointer-valued; addressable scalar AST routes such as `view->nested.values[i]` can legitimately produce pointers in pointer contexts, so mixed `pointer < scalar_field_read` was mistaken for cross-array pointer ordering. Scalar/scalar now uses `eval()`, pointer/pointer uses `eval_pointer()` plus `pointer_ordering()`, and mixed shapes retain `pointer ordering comparisons are not supported` without speculative evaluation. GCC/Clang and Cust return 130 on the warning-free same-array fixture. See `references/cust-ordering-classifier-modeling.md`.

- 2026-07-17: Equality must branch on `expr_is_pointer_value()` for both operands before runtime evaluation. The prior mixed branch speculatively called `eval_pointer()` on scalar operands and then recognized only raw `Expr::Number` literals, so C null-pointer constants such as `+0` and `(int)0` leaked `expected pointer expression`; wrapped nonzero integers and aggregate operands likewise leaked pointer-context errors. Evaluate mixed scalar operands exactly once: zero compares against `PointerValue::Null`, nonzero keeps `cannot compare pointer with nonzero integer`, and aggregate operands retain scalar-context diagnostics. GCC `-Werror=address` rejects direct known-array/null comparisons, so compiler-oracle fixtures should route the pointer through a helper. GCC/Clang and Cust return 129. See `references/cust-equality-classifier-modeling.md`.

- 2026-07-17: Nested named and anonymous-inner aggregate fields selected from a captured aggregate compound literal retain identity as literal root + nested field path + selected array field + element index. Capturing the enclosing compound literal's address once allows both fields to share one hidden root while supporting named and anonymous inner aggregate paths; initializer markers prove one-time evaluation. The RED fixture also showed `eval_equality()` must consult `expr_is_pointer_value()` before probing both operands with `eval_pointer()`: `StructPtrArrayGet` can represent a scalar nested array-field element but its pointer evaluator returns an address for pointer contexts, so speculative probing misclassified `root->inner.values[i] == n`. Scalar/scalar equality now evaluates directly, while pointer/nonzero diagnostics remain unchanged. GCC/Clang compile the warning-free same-root fixture and return 20 like Cust. Six recent field-backed fixtures were added to the canonical `c_compat` list after inspection showed the files existed but were not registered. See `references/cust-scalar-equality-pointer-classification.md`.

- 2026-07-16: Aggregate compound-literal array-field roots remain alive for the enclosing block when captured once into pointer variables; one/two-hop calls and arithmetic/indexed-address/conditional/comma wrappers preserve each hidden root's field/index identity. A marker in each initializer proves roots are not re-evaluated. Const-qualified aggregate compound literals require top-level `read_only` metadata on `Expr::AggregateLiteral` and `AddressOfAggregateLiteral`: merge it with field `is_const` only for aggregate-field slot/array qualification, not with a pointer-valued field's pointee qualification. The model's RED run exposed the missing metadata; GCC and Clang compile the warning-free C11 fixture and return 20 like Cust. See `references/cust-const-aggregate-compound-literal-fields.md`.

- 2026-07-16: Nested named holders and anonymous holder objects can share one returned-pointer alias model when storage identity is represented as containing-path + owner + selected field + element index. Anonymous objects and their pointer views must be declared in one declarator list so they share the same anonymous aggregate identity; helper signatures should mention only the selected element type. Capture path as a distinct weighted-storage dimension, and keep cross-path subtraction interpreter-only because unrelated native arrays do not define pointer difference. GCC and Clang compile the warning-free nested/anonymous fixture and return 36 like Cust. See `references/cust-field-backed-pointer-forwarding-models.md`.

- 2026-07-16: Returned field-backed pointers keep owner + field path + element index identity through caller variables and copied callee parameter slots. For ordered two-writer mutation, model the first replacement before the const-reader observation, then apply the second compound update before observing again; same-element aliases therefore compose both writes, while different fields/owners remain isolated. A position-weighted checksum over both fields in both objects catches wrong-path writes, and explicit caller equality/difference checks prove callee slot reassignment is local. GCC and Clang compile the warning-free scalar/struct/union fixture and return 36 like Cust. See `references/cust-field-backed-pointer-forwarding-models.md`.

- 2026-07-16: Pointer-returning forwarding helpers must preserve field-backed storage identity as containing object + field path + element index; direct versus arrow syntax and one/two copied parameter slots do not create new storage. Model conditional selection separately from its merged pointee qualification, apply arithmetic only to the index, and treat comma/right-operand and indexed-address wrappers as identity-preserving. Exact cross-field/root arithmetic remains interpreter-only because native C does not define unrelated pointer subtraction/ordering. GCC and Clang compile the warning-free scalar/struct/union fixture and return 160 like Cust. See `references/cust-model-based-field-backed-pointer-return-forwarding.md`.

- 2026-07-16: Qualification does not change field-backed pointer identity: direct `holder.items + i` and arrow `holder_view->items + i` routes retain the same containing owner, field path, and element index when passed as `T *writer` / `const T *reader`. Model mutation before observation, but keep different fields and different containing objects as independent roots even when indexes match. A position-weighted checksum catches writes to the wrong field; separate pointer differences prove caller slots retain their original field roots after callee-local reassignment. GCC and Clang compile the warning-free scalar/struct/union fixture and return 28 like Cust. See `references/cust-model-based-field-backed-mixed-qualification-pointer-parameters.md`.

- 2026-07-16: A mutable backing root may be passed simultaneously as `T *writer` and `const T *reader`; qualification changes the reader expression's write permissions without changing interpreter-owned storage identity. Model the writer mutation before the reader observation: same-element readers see the replacement, while distinct-index/cross-root readers retain their own values. Keep weighted final-storage and caller root/index checks separate from the observed value and callee-slot identity checks. GCC and Clang compile the warning-free scalar/struct/union fixture and return 63 like Cust. See `references/cust-model-based-mixed-qualification-pointer-parameter-aliasing.md`.

- 2026-07-16: Two pointer parameters preserve shared interpreter-owned storage identity independently of their copied local slots. Ordered writes must be modeled in call order: a same-element pair ends with the first replacement plus the second compound delta, same-root distinct indexes mutate separate elements, and cross-root pairs mutate separate backing arrays. Use a position- and root-weighted storage checksum rather than a plain sum so writes to the wrong element cannot accidentally pass; separately verify caller pointer identities after both callee slots are reassigned. GCC and Clang compile the warning-free scalar/struct/union fixture and return 54 like Cust. See `references/cust-model-based-pointer-parameter-aliasing.md`.

Avoid sampling only a PRNG's low bit at a fixed stride; the initial coverage counters exposed a one-sided sequence, so mutation-case root/hop choices use a higher bit and enforce minimum route counts. GCC and Clang compile the warning-free fixture and return 35 like Cust. See `references/cust-model-based-pointer-parameter-mutation.md`.

Fixed-seed modeling covers nested forwarding inside arithmetic, indexed-address, conditional, and comma expressions plus cross-root/bounds/out-of-scope cases. See `references/cust-model-based-pointer-return-function-boundaries.md`.

Const aggregate-array typedef compound literals propagate pointee `const`; mutable literals remain writable. - 2026-07-15: Embedded aggregate-array field pointer identity is determined by the containing storage owner plus the nested field path and element index; direct decay, `&field[i]`, and arrow access to the same owner/path must compare as the same array, while different owners or nested paths retain `cannot subtract pointers to different arrays`. Aggregate compound-literal fields use hidden literal storage identities and report `struct array pointer index ...` bounds errors, while variable-backed embedded fields report `struct array field pointer index ...`. A fixed-seed test-side model now checks these distinctions across named/anonymous struct/union containers, struct/union element types, const fields, and arithmetic/difference/conditional/comma wrappers. For `view->field`, a const containing-aggregate pointer qualifies the field slot but does not make the field pointer's pointee const; use the selected field's `points_to_const` metadata instead. - 2026-07-15: Pointer arithmetic must classify operand result shapes before evaluation rather than speculatively calling `eval_pointer()` and treating success/failure as type metadata. A wrapped scalar zero such as `(0 ? 2 : (marker += 1, 0))` is a valid arithmetic offset but speculative pointer evaluation sees its selected `0` as null; discarded errors can also replace an earlier `cannot subtract pointers to different arrays` with `expected pointer expression`. `eval_pointer_arithmetic()` now uses `expr_is_pointer_value()` first, evaluates only the selected pointer/scalar routes, and the classifier includes addressable literal and direct/arrow/aggregate pointer-field assignment results already accepted by `eval_pointer()`. See `references/cust-model-based-pointer-expression-properties.md`.

- 2026-07-15: Pointer result metadata must follow expression result shape consistently across `expr_is_pointer_value()`, `pointer_expr_pointee_type()`, and `pointer_expr_points_to_const()`. For addition, constness comes from the pointer-valued operand; for subtraction, only pointer-minus-scalar is pointer-valued and constness comes from its left base. OR-ing both operands incorrectly leaked qualification from const pointers consumed by a scalar difference into `values + (const_right - const_left)`. Metadata classification remains non-evaluating. Focused RED reproduced `cannot discard const qualifier from pointer target`; GREEN Cust and warning-free GCC/Clang return 9, while genuinely const bases and cross-array diagnostics remain protected. See `references/cust-pointer-difference-const-metadata.md`.

- 2026-07-15: `expr_is_pointer_value()` must classify the result of subtraction, not recursively propagate pointer-ness from either operand. Pointer-pointer subtraction produces a scalar difference, pointer-minus-scalar remains pointer-valued, scalar-minus-pointer remains invalid through the scalar evaluator, and pointer addition still uses pointer routing for one or two pointer operands so existing behavior/diagnostics stay intact. Splitting the shared Add/Sub classifier arm fixes larger additive, multiplicative, conditional, comma, and truthiness expressions while preserving `cannot subtract pointers to different arrays`. GCC/Clang warning-free fixtures return 42 like Cust. See `references/cust-pointer-difference-scalar-classification.md`. The then-confirmed const-metadata follow-up is completed in `references/cust-pointer-difference-const-metadata.md`.

Route aggregate pointee fields through `offset_array_pointer()`, keep scalar fields on the old helper, and infer arrow-field type/const metadata without evaluation. Pointer-slot `const` and a const containing-aggregate view do not qualify the pointed-to object; only `const T *` should reject mutable conversion. GCC/Clang warning-free fixtures return 110/20 like Cust. The run also exposed a separate classifier gap: a larger scalar additive expression containing pointer subtraction can be misclassified as pointer-valued; this is now the first follow-up backlog item. Cust already parsed `&p[i]` as `Expr::AddressOfArray` and preserved static pointee/const metadata, but runtime evaluation sent every pointer variable through the scalar-array helper; routing aggregate pointer variables through `indexed_struct_pointer()` first fixes the storage target while reusing existing bounds and out-of-scope safety.

Implementation decision: reject `*` immediately after every parsed direct array suffix in casts and shared `sizeof`/`_Alignof` type-name parsing, reporting `pointer-to-array casts/<operator> types are not supported` at the star and preserving multidimensional-array, function-array, pointer-array, and valid one-dimensional routes.

As a result, direct scalar, named/anonymous aggregate, and array-typedef forms fell through to a generic closing-parenthesis error. Thus `(int *(void))0`, `sizeof(int *(void))`, and `_Alignof(IntPtr(void))` reached generic delimiter errors. Implementation decision: reuse `reject_function_type_suffix(...)` after every supported pointer route (direct scalar/named/anonymous aggregate and pointer typedef) while retaining its existing `(*` exception for dedicated parenthesized-pointer diagnostics.
