Root cause: `parse_cast()` and `parse_sizeof_like_type_name()` guarded a function declarator immediately after the base type, but ordinary scalar and aggregate array suffix parsing consumed `[N]` and delegated the following `(` to a generic closing-parenthesis helper. Implementation decision: reuse `reject_function_type_suffix(...)` immediately after parsed array suffixes in casts, `sizeof`, and `_Alignof`; this retains the established function-type wording/location and does not alter valid array compound literals.

- 2026-07-11: No external documentation was needed for pointer-array `sizeof`/`_Alignof` diagnostics. Root cause: `parse_sizeof_like_type_name()` checked an array suffix after pointer typedef aliases but returned `SizeOfType::Pointer` immediately after direct scalar/named/anonymous aggregate `*`, leaving `[` for the generic closing-parenthesis helper. Implementation decision: add the same post-star `LBracket` guard to all three direct type-query branches, reporting `pointer array <operator> types are not supported` at `[` and preserving ordinary array type operands, post-star qualifiers, and the pointer-to-pointer boundary.

- 2026-07-11: Local `cc -std=c11 -Wall -Wextra -Werror -fsyntax-only` rejects `sizeof(restrict int)`, `_Alignof(restrict int)`, and `(restrict int)0` as invalid uses of `restrict`. Root cause: declaration parsing called `reject_leading_restrict_qualifier()`, but `parse_sizeof_like_type_name()` and `parse_cast()` consumed leading qualifiers directly, silently accepting `restrict` as no-op scalar metadata. Implementation decision: invoke the existing guard before those two direct type-name qualifier-consumption paths, preserving valid post-star pointer `restrict` syntax and the existing declaration/parameter/field diagnostic text.

- 2026-07-11: No external documentation was needed for unsupported array range designator diagnostics. Root cause: `...` lexes as three `Token::Dot` values; after a valid constant index, each shared array-designator parser called the generic closing-bracket helper, producing `expected ']' after array designator, found Dot`. Implementation decision: reuse the existing conservative three-dot lookahead (`starts_ellipsis`) in a post-index guard shared by bounded and unbounded array-designator parsing, reporting `array range designators are not supported` at the first dot. Exact-output interpreter coverage includes declared arrays, unsized array compound literals, and aggregate scalar-array field path designators; no GNU range-designator runtime support was added.

- 2026-07-11: No external documentation was needed for aggregate-initializer array-designator-start diagnostics. Root cause: `parse_struct_initializer()` permits dot-led field designators but, unlike array initializer paths, had no guard for a bare `Token::LBracket`, so `(struct Point){[}` reached scalar-expression parsing and emitted generic `expected expression, found LBracket`. Implementation decision: reject that token at the aggregate initializer element boundary with the existing contextual initializer wording, while preserving `[` designators in scalar/aggregate array initializer parsers and `.` field designators in aggregate initializers.

- 2026-07-11: No external documentation was needed for `sizeof` invalid-start diagnostics. Root cause: `reject_missing_sizeof_operand()` handled delimiter-like and selector/brace/bracket/question invalid starts together, so `sizeof([)`, `sizeof(?)`, `sizeof(:)`, `sizeof(.)`, `sizeof(->field)`, and `sizeof({1})` produced generic `found ...` diagnostics or fell through to primary-expression parsing. Implementation decision: keep delimiter-only missing operands (`sizeof()`, `sizeof(,)`, `sizeof;`) as `found ...`, but route bracket/question/colon/selector/brace starts to `expected sizeof operand before '<token>'`, matching adjacent `_Alignof`, `_Alignas`, `_Atomic(type-name)`, and integer-constant boundary style.

- 2026-07-11: No external documentation was needed for `_Atomic(type-name)` selector/colon invalid-start diagnostics. Root cause: `parse_decl_type_with_embedded_qualifiers()` handled `[`/`?`/`{`/keyword invalid starts contextually, then left `:`, `.`, and `->` in the generic delimiter/selector fallback. Implementation decision: reuse `integer_constant_invalid_start_or_selector_label()` for `_Atomic(type-name)` when the next token is not a valid type-name start, matching the existing parser-trust `before '<token>'` style used by `_Alignof`, `_Alignas`, and integer-constant boundaries while preserving valid bare `_Atomic` and `_Atomic(type-name)` parsing.
- 2026-07-11: No external documentation was needed for `_Alignas` / `_Static_assert` colon invalid-start diagnostics. Root cause: both operand guards handled delimiters, braces, brackets, selectors, and keyword invalid starts, but still delegated `Token::Colon` to generic expression parsing. Implementation decision: route both guards through `integer_constant_invalid_start_or_selector_label()` so colon shares the parser-trust `before ':'` diagnostic style already used in integer-constant boundaries, while leaving valid `_Alignas(type-name/expression)` and `_Static_assert(condition, message)` routes unchanged.

- 2026-07-10: No external documentation was needed for colon-token parameter and integer-constant parser-trust diagnostics. Root cause: the function-parameter guard covered commas, brackets, question, and braces but not semicolon/colon/selector starts, and shared integer-constant invalid-start labelling covered brace/selector starts but not colon starts. Implementation decision: add semicolon/colon/dot/arrow to initial and after-comma parameter-list guards while checking `...` before Dot/Arrow guards so variadic-parameter diagnostics remain targeted; include `Token::Colon` in `integer_constant_invalid_start_or_selector_label()` so fixed array lengths, array designator indexes, enum values, and switch case labels report contextual `before ':'` diagnostics.

- 2026-07-10: No external documentation was needed for subscript comma expressions. Root cause: `parse_index_expr()` parsed only `parse_logical_or()` before requiring `]`, so direct C subscript expressions such as `values[i++, 2]` failed with `expected ']' after array index, found Comma` even though Cust already supports comma expressions elsewhere and parenthesized comma expressions in indexes. Implementation decision: keep the existing first-operand/index-start path to preserve diagnostics like `values[0 = 3;`, then parse comma tails into `Expr::Comma` with normal comma-operator missing-RHS diagnostics. See `references/cust-subscript-comma-expressions.md`.

- 2026-07-10: No external documentation was needed for colon-token parser-trust diagnostics. Root cause: several route-local expression-boundary guards intentionally handled delimiters, brackets, selectors, braces, and keywords but omitted `Token::Colon`, so malformed forms such as `callee(:)`, `values[:]`, `int values[2] = {:}`, `return :;`, `if (:)`, `switch (:)`, and `return (:);` delegated to generic primary-expression parsing. Implementation decision: add narrow colon guards only at function-call, array-index, braced-initializer, return/control/switch, for-clause, and grouped-expression boundaries while preserving valid conditional-operator colon parsing and statement-label/case/default routing.

- 2026-07-10: No external documentation was needed for top-level invalid-start parser diagnostics. Root cause: `parse_program()` preserved exact unmatched delimiter and stray control-flow diagnostics, but otherwise returned the broad `unexpected token at top level: ...` fallback for malformed declaration-start tokens. Implementation decision: add a narrow file-scope guard for `Token::LBrace`, `Comma`, `Question`, `Dot`, and `Arrow` so malformed starts report `expected top-level declaration, found ...` without changing normal declaration/function routing.

- 2026-07-10: No external documentation was needed for brace-token `_Atomic`/parameter parser-trust diagnostics. Root cause: `_Atomic(type-name)` guarded `[`, `?`, and keyword/selector/delimiter starts but not `{`, and the initial function-parameter guard likewise omitted `{` even though after-comma parameter parsing already handled it. Implementation decision: add narrow `Token::LBrace` guards in `parse_decl_type_with_embedded_qualifiers()` for `_Atomic({)` and in `parse_params()` for leading `{` so `_Atomic({)`, `sizeof(_Atomic({))`, and `int add({ int b)` report contextual diagnostics without changing valid blocks or braced initializers.

- 2026-07-10: No external documentation was needed for brace-token parser-trust diagnostics. Root cause: `{` is valid as a statement-leading block and in supported initializer/compound-literal grammars, but malformed expression/type/integer-constant operand positions should not delegate `Token::LBrace` into generic primary/type parsing. Implementation decision: add narrow route-local `LBrace` guards for `_Alignas`, `_Static_assert`, `sizeof`, `_Alignof`, grouped expressions, array/index/integer-constant boundaries, control clauses, and operator operands so examples like `sizeof({1})`, `_Alignof({1})`, `values[{1}]`, `case {1}:`, and `return 1 ? {2} : 3` report contextual diagnostics while statement blocks and valid braced initializers remain unchanged.
- 2026-07-10: No external documentation was needed for selector-token integer-constant diagnostics. Root cause: parenthesized integer constants and ordinary expression routes already recognized malformed Dot/Arrow starts contextually, but array declarator lengths, array designator indexes, enum constant values, and switch case labels still delegated those selector tokens into generic integer-constant parsing. Implementation decision: add `integer_constant_invalid_start_or_selector_label()` and use it only in those integer-constant boundary guards so `int values[.field];`, `{[->field] = 1}`, `enum Bad { A = .field };`, and `case ->field:` report contextual `expected ... before '.'/'->'` diagnostics without changing expression-context selector-token wording.
- 2026-07-10: No external documentation was needed for selector-token array/string index diagnostics. Root cause: parser-trust guards covered Dot/Arrow starts in statement, return/operator/control/type-query/cast/function-call/initializer/grouped/unary routes, but `parse_index_expr()` still delegated malformed selector tokens inside array and string indexes to generic primary-expression parsing. Implementation decision: add narrow `Token::Dot` / `Token::Arrow` guards to `parse_index_expr()` so `values[.field]` and `"hi"[->field]` report contextual `expected array index expression...` diagnostics while preserving valid postfix selectors after real indexed expressions.

- 2026-07-09: No external documentation was needed for selector-token grouped-expression and unary-operand diagnostics. Root cause: parser-trust guards covered Dot/Arrow starts in statement, return/operator/control/type-query/cast/function-call/initializer routes, but grouped expression operands and prefix-unary operands still delegated malformed selector tokens to generic primary-expression parsing. Implementation decision: add narrow `Token::Dot` / `Token::Arrow` guards to the grouped-expression start check and `reject_missing_unary_operand(...)` while preserving valid postfix selector expressions after a real operand.
- 2026-07-09: No external documentation was needed for selector-token function-call/initializer diagnostics. Root cause: parser-trust guards covered Dot/Arrow starts in return/operator/control/type-query/cast routes, but function-call argument lists and braced initializer list starts still delegated malformed selector tokens to generic primary-expression parsing. The fix is route-local: treat Dot/Arrow as invalid call-argument starts and reject Arrow plus non-designator Dot in initializer element starts while preserving valid struct `.field` designators.
- 2026-07-09: No external documentation was needed for selector-token control/conditional expression diagnostics. Root cause: earlier parser-trust guards covered Dot/Arrow selector tokens in expression statements, return/operator RHS, `sizeof`, casts, `_Alignas`, and `_Static_assert`, but not in for-initializer starts, control-flow conditions, for increments, or conditional-operator branch operands. Malformed starts such as `for (.field; ; )`, `if (.field)`, `while (->field)`, `for (...; .field; ...)`, `for (...; ...; ->field)`, `return 1 ? .field : 2`, and `return 1 ? 2 : ->field` therefore fell through to generic primary-expression or for-increment fallbacks. Implementation decision: add narrow Dot/Arrow guards only at those expression-boundary start checks so valid postfix selector expressions after a real base expression remain unchanged.

- 2026-07-09: No external documentation was needed for qualifier-only declaration-type diagnostics. Root cause: declaration parsers consumed leading `const` / `volatile` / bare `_Atomic` qualifiers before asking the general type parser for a type, so malformed qualifier-only declarations such as `const global;`, `volatile local;`, `_Atomic value` parameters, aggregate fields, and typedef aliases lost the qualifier context and reported generic route-specific type errors. Implementation decision: have qualifier consumption optionally return the last consumed qualifier token, then reject non-type starts immediately in declaration/parameter/field/typedef contexts with `expected <context> after type qualifier '<qualifier>'...` while leaving valid qualified declarations and cast/sizeof/_Alignof qualifier diagnostics unchanged.

- 2026-07-09: No external documentation was needed for function-parameter keyword-start diagnostics. Root cause: `parse_params()` guarded delimiter, bracket, and `?` starts at the beginning of a parameter list and after separator commas, but delegated statement/control keywords such as `return` into `parse_decl_type(...)`, producing the generic `expected type, found Return`. Implementation decision: add narrow parameter-list guards for non-type keyword starts while preserving valid type-name starts, old-style identifier-list detection, variadic `...` diagnostics, and ordinary parameter parsing.

- 2026-07-09: No external documentation was needed for parenthesized integer-constant operand diagnostics. Root cause: `parse_integer_constant_primary()` delegated immediately to recursive integer-constant parsing after consuming `(`, so malformed `()`, `(,)`, `(:`, `(?)`, and `(return)` in enum initializers, array lengths, and switch labels produced generic `expected integer constant in parenthesized integer constant expression, found ...` messages. Implementation decision: add a narrow start-token guard for parenthesized integer-constant expressions while leaving valid parenthesized constants and post-left-operand comma-operator rejection unchanged.

- 2026-07-09: No external documentation was needed for `_Alignof` selector-token invalid-start diagnostics. Root cause: `reject_missing_alignof_type()` already guarded invalid `[` / `?` starts and keyword starts, but selector tokens `.` and `->` reached the generic non-type fallback and reported `expected _Alignof type, found Dot/Arrow`. Implementation decision: add narrow selector-token guards so malformed `_Alignof(.)` and `_Alignof(->field)` report contextual `expected _Alignof type before '.'/'->'` diagnostics while valid `_Alignof(type-name)` operands and integer-constant `_Alignof` folding remain unchanged.

- 2026-07-09: No external documentation was needed for `_Atomic(type-name)` invalid-start diagnostics. Root cause: `parse_decl_type` guarded empty/comma/delimiter `_Atomic(...)` arguments, but delegated invalid starts like `[` / `?` / statement keywords / selector tokens to generic type parsing. Implementation decision: add narrow `_Atomic` type-argument guards before `parse_decl_type_with_embedded_qualifiers(...)` so declaration, parameter, `sizeof`, cast, and aggregate-field routes preserve contextual `_Atomic` diagnostics while keeping valid bare `_Atomic` qualifiers and `_Atomic(type-name)` specifiers unchanged.

- 2026-07-09: No external documentation was needed for selector-token invalid starts in `_Alignas`, `_Static_assert`, `sizeof`, and cast operands. Root cause: those route-local operand guards handled delimiters, invalid `[`/`?` starts, and keyword starts, but still delegated leading `.` / `->` selector tokens into generic expression parsing. Implementation decision: include `Token::Dot` and `Token::Arrow` in the route-local guards so malformed `_Alignas(.)`, `_Static_assert(->field, "msg")`, `sizeof(.)`, and `(int)->field` report contextual diagnostics while valid postfix selector use after aggregate/pointer expressions remains unchanged.

- 2026-07-09: No external documentation was needed for braced scalar initializer invalid-start diagnostics. Root cause: `parse_scalar_initializer_expr(...)` reused the braced initializer missing-element guard after `{`, which intentionally leaves `[` available for array/aggregate designators in other initializer contexts and does not classify selector tokens such as `.`/`->`; scalar braced initializers cannot use those designators, so malformed operands fell through to generic primary-expression parsing. Implementation decision: route scalar braced initializer operands through the narrower invalid-value guard and include selector tokens in that guard while keeping aggregate/array initializer designator starts unchanged.
- 2026-07-08: No external documentation was needed for integer-constant unary operand diagnostics. Root cause: parser-folded integer constant expressions consumed unary `+`, `-`, `~`, and `!` before recursively parsing the operand, so malformed forms such as `enum Bad { VALUE = + };`, `int values[-];`, `case !:`, and `~return` lost the operator context and reported a generic integer-constant error. Implementation decision: add a narrow post-unary guard in `parse_integer_constant_unary(...)` so enum initializers, fixed array lengths, and switch case labels report contextual `expected integer constant after unary operator '<op>'...` diagnostics while preserving supported unary integer constant expressions.

- 2026-07-08: No external documentation was needed for integer-constant cast operand diagnostics. Root cause: parser-folded integer constant expressions shared scalar cast parsing, but after `(int)`/`(char)` consumed the cast type they delegated missing or malformed operands such as `}`, `]`, `:`, and `return` to generic integer-constant parsing. Implementation decision: add a narrow post-cast guard in `parse_integer_constant_cast(...)` so enum initializers, fixed array lengths, and switch case labels report contextual `expected integer constant after cast...` diagnostics while preserving supported scalar casts in integer constant expressions.

- 2026-07-08: No external documentation was needed for dot/arrow invalid-start expression diagnostics. Root cause: statement, return, comma, assignment, and binary RHS invalid-start guards handled delimiters, `[`/`?`, and keyword starts but still let leading `.` and `->` selector tokens fall through to generic statement or primary-expression errors. Implementation decision: treat `Token::Dot` and `Token::Arrow` as contextual malformed expression-start tokens in those focused guards so invalid selector starts report source-located `expected expression...` diagnostics while valid postfix `.`/`->` selectors remain unchanged.

- 2026-07-08: No external documentation was needed for typedef keyword invalid-start expression diagnostics. Root cause: grouped-expression parsing and declaration-initializer guards handled delimiters, invalid punctuation starts, and several keyword-start routes elsewhere, but still delegated `return (typedef);` and `int value = typedef;` into generic expression parsing. Implementation decision: reuse the shared invalid-start keyword classifier in `parse_primary()` grouped-expression handling and `reject_missing_declaration_initializer_expr(...)` so these malformed programs report contextual source-located diagnostics while valid typedef declarations and expression starts remain unchanged.

- 2026-07-08: No external documentation was needed for expression-list keyword invalid-start diagnostics. Root cause: several expression-list and operand helpers had delimiter/`[`/`?` guards but still delegated statement/type keywords into generic expression parsing, so forms such as `take(return)`, `values[int]`, `int values[2] = {return};`, `return 1 + return;`, `value = return;`, and `return 1 ? return : 2;` reported `expected expression, found ...`. Implementation decision: reuse the shared keyword label classifier in function-call argument, array-index, braced-initializer element, comma/assignment/binary RHS, and conditional-operand guards so these malformed programs get contextual source-located diagnostics while preserving valid expression starts.
- 2026-07-08: No external documentation was needed for `_Alignas` and `_Static_assert` keyword invalid-start diagnostics. Root cause: both declaration-specifier `_Alignas(...)` and `_Static_assert(...)` guarded delimiter and invalid punctuation starts, but delegated statement/control keywords such as `return` into generic expression parsing. Implementation decision: reuse the shared keyword label classifier so malformed `_Alignas(return) int value;` and `_Static_assert(return, "condition required");` report contextual source-located diagnostics while preserving valid `_Alignas(type-name)`, `_Alignas(expression)`, and `_Static_assert` parsing.
- 2026-07-08: No external documentation was needed for `_Alignof` invalid-start diagnostics. Root cause: ordinary `parse_alignof()` and integer-constant `_Alignof` folding both checked only `is_type_name_start()`, so invalid type-start tokens such as `[`, `?`, and statement/control keywords reported generic `expected _Alignof type, found ...` diagnostics. Implementation decision: share a narrow `_Alignof` type-start guard across both routes; preserve existing `_Alignof()` / `_Alignof(,)` messages and valid `_Alignof(type-name)` behavior while reporting contextual `expected _Alignof type before '<token>'` for malformed starts.
- 2026-07-08: No external documentation was needed for `sizeof` invalid-start diagnostics. Root cause: `reject_missing_sizeof_operand()` guarded delimiter and invalid-start punctuation, but `parse_sizeof()` let statement/type keywords in expression-operand positions such as `sizeof return`, `sizeof int`, and `sizeof(return)` fall through to generic expression parsing. Implementation decision: pass whether parenthesized type names are allowed into the guard; preserve valid `sizeof(int)` / `sizeof(struct T)` routes while reporting `expected sizeof operand before '<token>'` for unparenthesized type keywords and statement/control keywords.

- 2026-07-08: No external documentation was needed for cast-operand invalid-start diagnostics. Root cause: `reject_missing_cast_operand()` guarded delimiter/terminator tokens after scalar, pointer, and void casts, but omitted invalid operand starts such as `[` / `?` and statement/type keywords. Implementation decision: align the cast-operand guard with other expression operand diagnostics so malformed casts report `expected expression after cast...` at the offending token while preserving valid casts and compound literals.

- 2026-07-08: No external documentation was needed for grouped-expression invalid-start diagnostics. Root cause: `parse_primary()` already guarded delimiter/terminator starts after `(` for grouped expressions, but let invalid operand starts `[` and `?` delegate into generic expression parsing. Implementation decision: add `LBracket` and `Question` to the grouped-expression operand guard so malformed forms like `return ([);` and `return (?);` report source-located `expected grouped expression...` diagnostics while preserving valid casts, grouped expressions, and existing missing-closing-paren behavior.

- 2026-07-07: No external documentation was needed for `for` initializer invalid-start diagnostics. Root cause: `parse_for()` had contextual handling for declaration initializers, assignment/expression initializers, and statement-only control-flow keywords, but invalid expression-start tokens such as `[`, `?`, and `,` still fell to the generic `unexpected token in for initializer` fallback. Implementation decision: add a narrow `for` initializer guard for these tokens so malformed forms report source-located `expected expression after for initializer, found ...` diagnostics while preserving valid empty/declaration/expression initializers and existing statement-only control-flow messages.

- 2026-07-07: No external documentation was needed for return/control keyword invalid-start diagnostics. Root cause: return expressions and control-flow condition/increment parsers guarded delimiter starts plus `[`/`?`, but delegated declaration/type/control keywords into generic expression parsing (or the generic for-increment fallback). Implementation decision: reuse the shared keyword label classifier for expression-context guards so malformed `return int;`, `if (return)`, `while (int)`, `do ... while (struct)`, and `for` condition/increment keyword starts report source-located `expected expression after <context> before '<keyword>'` diagnostics while preserving valid expression starts.

- 2026-07-07: No external documentation was needed for array-designator keyword invalid-start diagnostics. Root cause: `parse_array_designator_index_with_context(...)` and `parse_unbounded_array_designator_index()` guarded delimiter starts and invalid operand-start `[`/`?`, but still delegated declaration/type/control keywords into generic integer-constant-expression parsing. Implementation decision: reuse the shared integer-constant invalid-start label guard for bounded and unbounded designator indexes so malformed forms like `{[int] = 1}`, `(int[]){[return] = 1}`, and `struct Point points[2] = {[struct] = {1, 2}}` report `expected array designator index before '<token>'` at the keyword.

- 2026-07-07: No external documentation was needed for expression-statement invalid-start diagnostics. Root cause: `parse_stmt()` routed expression-capable statement starts through `parse_expr_stmt_with_semi(...)`, but malformed statement-leading tokens such as `[` / `?` / `,` fell to the generic statement fallback. Implementation decision: add a narrow `parse_stmt()` guard for `Comma | Colon | LBracket | Question` that reports `expected expression statement, found ...` without changing unmatched `)`/`]` diagnostics or block-ending `}` handling.

- 2026-07-07: No external documentation was needed for enum/switch integer-constant keyword invalid-start diagnostics. Root cause: enum constant value and switch `case` label parsers guarded invalid operand-start `[`/`?`, but still delegated declaration/type/control keywords into generic integer-constant-expression parsing. Implementation decision: rename the array-length keyword classifier to a shared integer-constant invalid-start label helper, reuse it for enum values and switch cases, and keep valid integer-constant starts such as `sizeof(...)` and `_Alignof(...)` outside the guard.

- 2026-07-07: No external documentation was needed for unary-operator invalid-start diagnostics. Root cause: `reject_missing_unary_operand(...)` guarded delimiter/terminator starts after prefix unary operators (`!`, `~`, `++`, `--`, unary `+`/`-`, `*`, `&`) but let invalid operand-start tokens such as `[`/`?` and statement/type keywords fall through to generic expression parsing. Implementation decision: extend the shared unary operand guard so malformed forms like `return ![;`, `return *?;`, and `return &return;` report source-located `expected expression after unary operator '<op>'...` diagnostics while preserving valid expression starts such as `sizeof(...)`, `_Alignof(...)`, grouped expressions, literals, identifiers, and casts.

- 2026-07-07: No external documentation was needed for array-length keyword/statement-token invalid-start diagnostics. Root cause: `expect_array_len()` guarded delimiter-only starts, `?`, nested `[`, `[*]`, non-constant identifiers, and comma expressions, but delegated declaration/type/control keywords into the generic integer-constant-expression parser. Malformed fixed array lengths such as `int values[int];`, function parameters `char text[char]`, aggregate fields `int values[struct];`, typedef aliases `typedef int Values[return];`, and type-name lengths `sizeof(int[while])` therefore reported `expected array length, found ...`. Implementation decision: add a shared array-length invalid-start keyword label guard before integer-constant parsing, but deliberately leave `sizeof` and `_Alignof` out of the guard because they are valid integer constant expression starts in Cust's supported array-length grammar.

- 2026-07-07: No external documentation was needed for switch case-label invalid-start diagnostics. Root cause: `parse_switch_case_value(...)` delegated invalid operand-start tokens `[` and `?` into the generic integer-constant-expression parser, so malformed labels such as `case [:` and `case ?:` reported `expected integer constant after switch case, found LBracket/Question` instead of the route-local `before ...` parser-trust style. Implementation decision: guard `LBracket` and `Question` immediately after `case` and report source-located `expected integer constant after switch case before '['/'?'` diagnostics.

- 2026-07-07: No external documentation was needed for enum constant value invalid-start diagnostics. Root cause: `parse_enum_constant_value(...)` delegated invalid operand-start tokens `[` and `?` into the generic integer-constant-expression parser, so malformed enum definitions such as `enum Bad { FIRST = [?] };` and `enum Bad { FIRST = ? };` reported `expected integer constant after enum constant '=', found LBracket/Question` instead of matching the route-local `before ...` parser-trust style. Implementation decision: guard `LBracket` and `Question` immediately after enum constant `=` and report source-located `expected integer constant after enum constant '=' before '['/'?'` diagnostics.

- 2026-07-07: No external documentation was needed for array-designator invalid-start diagnostics. Root cause: `parse_array_designator_index_with_context(...)` and `parse_unbounded_array_designator_index()` guarded delimiter starts such as `]`, `;`, `,`, and `}`, but delegated invalid operand-start tokens `[` and `?` into the generic integer-constant-expression parser. Implementation decision: add bounded and unbounded designator guards so malformed forms like `.values[[] = 1`, `.values[?] = 1`, `(int[]){[[] = 1}`, and `(int[]){[?] = 1}` report source-located `expected array designator index before '['/'?'` diagnostics.

- 2026-07-06: No external documentation was needed for function-parameter invalid-start diagnostics. Root cause: `parse_params()` guarded leading comma and `]` list-boundary mistakes, plus the same tokens after a separator comma, but let invalid type-start tokens `[` and `?` fall into generic declaration-type parsing. Implementation decision: add `LBracket` and `Question` to the initial and after-comma function-parameter guards so malformed forms like `int add([ int b)` and `int add(int a,? int b)` report source-located contextual parameter diagnostics.

- 2026-07-06: No external documentation was needed for declaration-specifier/assertion invalid-start diagnostics. Root cause: `_Alignas(...)` and `_Static_assert(...)` guards covered delimiter/terminator starts (`)`, `,`, `;`, `]`, `}`, EOF) but let invalid operand-start tokens `[` and `?` delegate into generic expression parsing. Implementation decision: add `LBracket` and `Question` to both route-local guards so malformed forms like `_Alignas([)` and `_Static_assert(?, "condition required")` report source-located contextual diagnostics.

- 2026-07-06: No external documentation was needed for array-length invalid-start diagnostics. Root cause: `expect_array_len()` had targeted guards for delimiter-only starts (`]`, `)`, `;`, `}`), star-VLA forms, non-constant identifiers, and comma expressions, but let invalid operand-start tokens `?` and nested `[` fall into the generic integer-constant parser. Implementation decision: add shared array-length guards for `Question` and `LBracket` so malformed forms like `int values[?];` and `sizeof(int[[)` report source-located `expected array length before '?'/'['` diagnostics across declarations, parameters, fields, typedefs, and type-name routes.

- 2026-07-06: No external documentation was needed for operator RHS invalid-start diagnostics. Root cause: comma, assignment, binary, and conditional operator RHS guards covered delimiter/terminator starts such as `;`, `)`, `]`, `}`, `,`, and `:`, but let invalid operand-start tokens `[` and `?` delegate to generic expression parsing. Implementation decision: include `LBracket` and `Question` in the relevant operator operand guards so malformed `return 1 + [;`, `value = [;`, `value += ?;`, `return 1, [;`, `return 1 ? [ : 2;`, and `return 1 ? 2 : [;` report source-located contextual operator diagnostics while preserving valid operator parsing.

- 2026-07-06: No external documentation was needed for return-expression invalid-start diagnostics. Root cause: `reject_missing_return_expr()` guarded delimiter/terminator starts such as `,`, `)`, `]`, `}`, and EOF after `return`, but let invalid operand-start tokens `[` and `?` delegate to generic expression parsing. Implementation decision: include `LBracket` and `Question` in the return-expression guard so malformed `return [;` and `return ?;` report source-located `expected expression after return...` diagnostics while preserving valid value returns and empty `return;`.

- 2026-07-06: No external documentation was needed for braced-initializer designator-value invalid-start diagnostics. Root cause: the shared braced-initializer guard had to allow `[` as a valid array designator start at element boundaries, so after designator `=` routes such as `[0] = ?` and `.x = [?]` still delegated to generic expression parsing. Implementation decision: keep the element-boundary guard permissive for `[` designators but add a value-position guard after array/field designator assignment tokens that rejects `[`/`?` with contextual `expected initializer element in ... initializer` diagnostics.

- 2026-07-06: No external documentation was needed for `for` clause invalid-start diagnostics. Root cause: `parse_for()` parsed non-empty conditions directly with `parse_expr()` and let invalid operand-start tokens like `[`/`?` fall into generic expression diagnostics, while the increment clause only produced `unexpected token in for increment` for the same malformed starts. Implementation decision: reuse `reject_missing_control_condition_expr("for condition")` before parsing non-empty `for` conditions and add a narrow increment guard for `[`/`?` so malformed `for (init; [; inc)` and `for (init; cond; [)` report source-located contextual `expected expression after for condition/increment` diagnostics.

- 2026-07-06: No external documentation was needed for control-flow invalid-start condition diagnostics. Root cause: `reject_missing_control_condition_expr(...)` and `reject_missing_switch_expr()` guarded delimiter/terminator starts such as `)`, `;`, `}`, and EOF after the opening condition parenthesis, but let invalid operand-start tokens `[` and `?` delegate into expression parsing, producing generic `expected expression, found LBracket/Question` for forms such as `if ([)`, `while (?)`, `do { } while ([);`, and `switch ([)`. Added those tokens to the shared control-condition and switch-expression guards in `src/lib.rs`, with exact-output coverage in `tests/interpreter.rs`.

- 2026-07-06: No external documentation was needed for declaration-initializer invalid-start diagnostics. Root cause: `reject_missing_declaration_initializer_expr(...)` guarded delimiter/terminator starts such as `;`, `,`, `)`, `]`, and `}`, but let invalid operand-start tokens `[` and `?` delegate into expression parsing after `=`, producing generic `expected expression, found LBracket/Question` for forms such as `int value = [?];` and `int *slot = ?;`. Added those tokens to the shared declaration-initializer guard in `src/lib.rs`, with exact-output coverage in `tests/interpreter.rs`.

- 2026-07-06: No external documentation was needed for misplaced array/string index diagnostics. Root cause: `parse_index_expr()` had contextual guards for delimiter/terminator-leading missing indexes such as `values[]`, `values[;`, and `values[}`, but let invalid operand-start tokens `[` and `?` delegate into expression parsing, producing generic `expected expression, found LBracket/Question` for `values[[)` and `"hi"[?]`. Added those tokens to the shared index-expression guard in `src/lib.rs`, with exact-output coverage in `tests/interpreter.rs`.

- 2026-07-05: No external documentation was needed for comma-leading array designator index diagnostics. Root cause: `parse_array_designator_index_with_context(...)` and `parse_unbounded_array_designator_index()` special-cased empty `[]`, semicolon, and brace starts, but `[ , ]` forms such as `.values[,]` and `(int[]){[,] = 1}` delegated into generic integer-constant-expression parsing and reported `expected array designator index, found Comma`. Added route-local comma guards in `src/lib.rs` so these report `expected array designator index before ','` at the delimiter, with exact-output coverage in `tests/interpreter.rs`.

- 2026-07-05: No external documentation was needed for delimiter-only array designator index diagnostics. Root cause: `parse_array_designator_index_with_context(...)` and `parse_unbounded_array_designator_index()` only special-cased empty `[]`; semicolon and brace starts such as `.values[;]`, `.values[}`, `(int[]){[;] = 1}`, and `(int[]){[} = 1}` delegated into generic integer-constant-expression parsing and reported `expected array designator index, found ...`. Added route-local guards in `src/lib.rs` so these report `expected array designator index before ';'` / `before '}'` at the delimiter, with exact-output coverage in `tests/interpreter.rs`.

- 2026-07-05: No external documentation was needed for misplaced function-call argument diagnostics. Root cause: `parse_call_args()` had contextual guards for missing delimiter-leading arguments such as `,`, `;`, `]`, and braces, but let invalid operand-start tokens `[` and `?` delegate into assignment-expression parsing, producing generic `expected expression, found ...` for `take([)` / `take(?)` and after-comma forms. Added those tokens to the initial and after-comma call-argument guards in `src/lib.rs`, with exact-output coverage in `tests/interpreter.rs`.

- 2026-07-05: No external documentation was needed for missing grouped-expression operand diagnostics. Root cause: `parse_primary()` consumed `(` and immediately delegated delimiter-leading grouped expressions such as `()`, `(, 1)`, `(;`, and `(]` to generic expression parsing, producing `expected expression, found ...`. Added a narrow grouped-expression delimiter guard in `src/lib.rs` so these malformed operands report `expected grouped expression, found ...` at the offending token while preserving existing missing-closing-paren diagnostics.

- 2026-07-05: No external documentation was needed for misplaced `sizeof` operand diagnostics. Root cause: `reject_missing_sizeof_operand()` treated delimiters/terminators such as `)` and `;` as missing operands, but let misplaced starter tokens `[` and `?` delegate to generic expression parsing, producing `expected expression, found LBracket/Question` for malformed `sizeof([)` / `sizeof(?)`. Added those tokens to the shared guard in `src/lib.rs` so the parser reports `expected sizeof operand, found ...` at the offending token.

- 2026-07-05: No external documentation was needed for aggregate-array field path designators. Root cause: `parse_struct_designator_after_dot(...)` treated `[` after a field name as scalar-array-only, so C-compatible aggregate-array field paths such as `.points[1].x = 7` and `.numbers[1].tag = 'C'` failed with `field 'points' is not an array for path designator` even though Cust already supported aggregate-array storage and scalar array-field path designators. Added `StructFieldType::StructArray` routing in `src/lib.rs` and changed aggregate-array field initializer application to merge designated element-field updates into existing element storage instead of rebuilding the whole field per partial designator.
- 2026-07-05: No external documentation was needed for semicolon/brace delimiter-only array-length diagnostics. Root cause: `expect_array_len()` had targeted checks for `]` and `)` after `[`, but delegated `;` and `}` to the generic integer-constant-expression parser, producing `expected array length, found Semi/RBrace` in malformed declaration/type-name routes. Added shared guards in `src/lib.rs` so these terminators report `expected array length before ';'` / `expected array length before '}'` at the delimiter, with focused coverage in `tests/interpreter.rs`.

- 2026-07-05: No external documentation was needed for missing control-flow condition diagnostics. Root cause: `parse_if`, `parse_while`, `parse_do_while`, and `parse_switch` consumed the opening parenthesis and then delegated delimiter-only conditions to generic expression parsing, producing `expected expression` instead of contextual parser-trust diagnostics. Added shared guards for `if`/`while`/`do-while` plus a switch-specific guard in `src/lib.rs` and exact-output coverage in `tests/interpreter.rs`.

- 2026-07-05: No external documentation was needed for missing `_Atomic(type-name)` specifier diagnostics. Root cause: `parse_decl_type_with_embedded_qualifiers(...)` consumed `_Atomic(` and delegated delimiter-leading missing type arguments to generic type parsing, so `_Atomic()` / `_Atomic(,)` reported `expected type, found ...` rather than a contextual specifier diagnostic. Added a guarded `_Atomic(...)` argument parser and cross-route regression coverage in `tests/interpreter.rs`.

- 2026-07-04: No external documentation was needed for qualifier-only `sizeof`/`_Alignof` type diagnostics. Root cause: `parse_sizeof_like_type_name(...)` consumed leading non-`const` type qualifiers such as `volatile` and bare `_Atomic` before delegating to generic declaration-type parsing, so malformed forms like `sizeof(volatile)` and `_Alignof(_Atomic)` lost the qualifier context and reported only `expected type, found RParen`. Implementation decision: preserve the first leading qualifier token before consuming qualifiers and, when no type name follows, report `expected sizeof/_Alignof type after type qualifier '<qualifier>', found ...` at the missing type location while preserving valid qualified type-query parsing.

- 2026-07-04: No external documentation was needed for qualifier-only cast type diagnostics. Root cause: `parse_cast()` consumed leading cast type qualifiers before delegating to generic type parsing, so malformed forms like `(const);`, `(volatile);`, and `(_Atomic);` lost the qualifier context and reported only `expected type, found RParen`. Implementation decision: preserve the leading qualifier token before consuming qualifiers and, when no cast type follows, report `expected cast type after type qualifier '<qualifier>', found ...` at the missing type location while preserving valid qualified casts and existing missing-cast-operand diagnostics.

- 2026-07-04: No external documentation was needed for missing return-expression diagnostics. Root cause: `parse_return()` correctly accepted `return;` as an empty return statement but delegated delimiter-leading malformed value returns such as `return ,;`, `return );`, and `return }` directly to expression parsing, producing the generic `expected expression, found ...` diagnostic. Implementation decision: add a narrow parser-local guard after the empty-`;` check and before `parse_expr()`, reporting `expected expression after return, found ...` at the offending delimiter while preserving void empty returns and existing return-shape diagnostics.

- 2026-07-04: No external documentation was needed for unsupported `void` object and typedef diagnostics. Root cause: parser routes treated any top-level/local `void` token as the start of a function declaration, so `void value;` fell through to `expected '(' after function name`, aggregate fields fell through to generic type parsing, and `typedef void Void;` reported `expected type, found Void`. Implementation decision: add narrow parser guards for non-function `void` declarations at file/block scope, aggregate field lists, and typedef parsing, reporting `void object declarations are not supported` or `void typedef aliases are not supported` at the `void` keyword while preserving supported `void` functions, empty `void` parameter lists, `(void)expr` casts, and existing `void *` diagnostics.

- 2026-07-04: No external documentation was needed for scalar compound-literal missing-initializer diagnostics. Audit finding: `(int){}`, `(char){,}`, and `(int){;}` already route scalar compound literals through `parse_scalar_initializer_expr("scalar compound literal")`, which uses the shared braced-scalar initializer delimiter guard and reports `expected initializer element in braced scalar initializer for scalar compound literal, found ...` with source locations. Implementation decision: add focused regression coverage only; no production-code change was needed.

- 2026-07-04: No external documentation was needed for missing cast-expression operand diagnostics. Root cause: scalar, pointer, pointer-typedef, anonymous aggregate pointer, and `(void)` cast routes consumed the complete cast type and then delegated delimiter-leading operands such as `(int);`, `(int *) ;`, and `(void);` directly to `parse_unary()`, producing generic `expected expression, found Semi` messages. Implementation decision: add a narrow `reject_missing_cast_operand()` guard after the closing cast-type parenthesis and before operand parsing for all evaluated cast routes, reporting `expected expression after cast, found ...` at the offending delimiter while preserving valid cast and compound-literal routing.

- 2026-07-04: No external documentation was needed for `_Alignas` missing-argument diagnostics. Root cause: `consume_alignment_specifiers()` consumed `_Alignas(` and delegated delimiter-leading arguments such as `_Alignas()` / `_Alignas(,)` / `_Alignas(;` directly to assignment-expression parsing, producing generic `expected expression, found ...` diagnostics. Implementation decision: add a narrow delimiter/terminator guard before deciding between type-name and assignment-expression arguments so malformed declaration specifiers report `expected _Alignas argument, found ...` at the offending token while preserving valid `_Alignas(type-name)` and `_Alignas(expression)` no-op metadata behavior.

- 2026-07-04: No external documentation was needed for `_Static_assert` missing-argument diagnostics. Root cause: `parse_static_assert()` consumed `_Static_assert(` and delegated delimiter-leading conditions such as `_Static_assert(, "msg")` directly to assignment-expression parsing, producing the generic `expected expression, found Comma`. Implementation decision: add a narrow delimiter/terminator guard before parsing the condition and report `expected _Static_assert condition, found ...` at the offending token, while preserving assignment-precedence condition parsing so the separator comma remains available and keeping the existing string-literal message diagnostic for `_Static_assert(1, )`.

- 2026-07-04: No external documentation was needed for non-`char` string literal initializer type-mismatch source locations. Root cause: fixed and inferred array string-initializer parser routes, plus scalar array compound-literal string entries, consumed the `Token::StringLiteral` and then returned `CustError::new(...)` when the element type was not `char`, losing the token location. Implementation decision: capture `peek_located()` before advancing and report the existing `string literal initializer requires char array ...` messages via `Parser::error_at(...)` for fixed arrays, inferred arrays, aggregate-field array reuse, and array compound literals.

- 2026-07-04: No external documentation was needed for inferred array missing-initializer diagnostics. Root cause: after parsing `[] =`, scalar and aggregate inferred-array declaration routes delegated directly to the compound-literal initializer parsers, so delimiter-leading forms such as `int values[] = ;` and `struct Point points[] = ;` reported generic `expected '{' after ... compound literal initializer`. Implementation decision: reuse the shared declaration-initializer delimiter guard immediately after the `=` in all inferred scalar and aggregate array declaration routes, including declaration-list tails, so the diagnostic stays at the declaration level with source location metadata.

- 2026-07-04: No external documentation was needed for source-located too-long char-array string initializer diagnostics. Root cause: `parse_string_literal_array_initializer(...)` and the char array compound-literal string path detected overlong string literal initializers after consuming the token, then returned unlocated `CustError::new(...)` diagnostics. Implementation decision: preserve the `LocatedToken` before advancing and report via `Parser::error_at(...)` for fixed char array declarations, aggregate char-array field initializers, and fixed-size char array compound literals, preserving existing exact message text with added line/column metadata.

- 2026-07-03: No external documentation was needed for missing braced scalar initializer diagnostics. Root cause: `parse_scalar_initializer_expr(...)` consumed `{` and delegated immediately to assignment-expression parsing, so delimiter-leading scalar initializers such as `int value = {,};`, `int value = {};`, and scalar aggregate-field entries like `.x = {,}` reported generic `expected expression, found ...`. Implementation decision: reuse the braced-initializer delimiter guard immediately after the scalar initializer opening brace with context `braced scalar initializer for ...`, preserving valid scalar braced initializer expressions and optional trailing commas.

- 2026-07-03: No external documentation was needed for missing braced initializer element diagnostics. Root cause: array, struct/union, and aggregate-array initializer loops delegated directly to scalar/aggregate expression parsing when a delimiter appeared where the next element should start, so malformed forms such as `int values[2] = {, 1}`, `[0] = ,`, `struct Point p = {, 1}`, `.x = ,`, and `struct Point points[2] = {, {1, 2}}` reported generic `expected expression, found Comma`. Implementation decision: add a shared parser-local delimiter/terminator guard for braced initializer element starts and designator values, returning `expected initializer element in ... initializer, found ...` while preserving empty `{}` and trailing comma forms.

- 2026-07-03: No external documentation was needed for missing struct field-name diagnostic coverage. The audited parser routes for direct `.` and pointer `->` field selectors already call the shared `expect_ident_after(...)` helper with selector-specific context strings, so malformed `p.;`, `p.];`, `q->;`, and `q->];` report exact `expected struct field name after '.'/'->', found ...` diagnostics with source locations. Implementation decision: add focused regression coverage only; no production-code change was needed.

- 2026-07-03: No external documentation was needed for misplaced closing-bracket function call/parameter diagnostics. Root cause: the previous parser guards for missing call arguments and missing function parameters covered commas, semicolons, braces, EOF, and trailing `)`, but omitted `Token::RBracket`, so malformed sources such as `first(]` and `int add(] int b)` delegated into generic expression/type parsing. Implementation decision: include `RBracket` in the missing-initial and after-comma guards for call arguments and function parameters, preserving valid empty calls/parameter lists and existing trailing-comma diagnostics.

- 2026-07-03: No external documentation was needed for malformed function-parameter comma diagnostics. Root cause: `parse_params()` let a leading `Token::Comma` fall through to `parse_const_qualified_decl_type("parameter type")`, producing the generic `expected type, found Comma`, and its after-comma guard omitted `Token::Comma`, so doubled separators produced the same generic type error. Implementation decision: add a narrow leading-comma guard that reports `expected function parameter, found Comma` and include `Token::Comma` in the existing after-comma missing-parameter guard, preserving `f(void)`, valid parameters, and trailing-comma diagnostics.

- 2026-07-02: No external documentation was needed for comma-leading function-call argument diagnostics. Root cause: the previous missing-initial-argument guard in `parse_call_args()` handled semicolon/brace/EOF starts such as `first(;` but not `Token::Comma`, so malformed `first(,)` and `add(, 2)` delegated to assignment-expression parsing and reported generic `expected expression, found Comma`. Implementation decision: include `Token::Comma` in the initial call-argument delimiter guard so leading comma reports `expected function call argument, found Comma`, while preserving empty `f()` and existing trailing-comma `expected function call argument after ','` diagnostics.

- 2026-07-02: No external documentation was needed for malformed function-call missing-initial-argument diagnostics. Root cause: after parsing an identifier followed by `(`, `parse_call_args()` only special-cased empty `)` and comma-following missing arguments; delimiter starts such as `first(;` and `first(}` delegated directly to assignment-expression parsing and produced generic `expected expression, found Semi/RBrace`. Implementation decision: add a narrow delimiter/terminator guard at the start of `parse_call_args()` that reports `expected function call argument, found ...` while preserving valid zero-argument calls (`f()`) and existing trailing-comma diagnostics (`f(1,)`).

- 2026-07-02: No external documentation was needed for array-index missing-expression diagnostics. Root cause: `parse_index_expr()` delegated directly to logical-or expression parsing after `[` was consumed, so empty or delimiter-only indexes such as `values[]`, `values[;`, `"hi"[]`, and `values[}` fell through to generic primary-expression messages like `expected expression, found RBracket/Semi/RBrace`. Implementation decision: add a narrow delimiter/terminator guard at the start of `parse_index_expr()` and report `expected array index expression, found ...` at the offending token while preserving valid array, pointer, string, and aggregate-array indexing semantics.

- 2026-07-02: No external documentation was needed for unary-operator missing-operand diagnostics. Root cause: `parse_unary()` consumed prefix operators (`++`, `--`, unary `+`, unary `-`, `~`, `!`, dereference `*`, and address-of `&`) and recursively delegated to `parse_unary()`, so malformed forms such as `return !;`, `return ~);`, `return ++];`, `return *}`, and `return &;` fell through to generic primary-expression messages like `expected expression, found Semi/RParen/RBracket/RBrace`. Implementation decision: preserve the consumed prefix operator `LocatedToken`, share a narrow post-operator delimiter/terminator check, and report `expected expression after unary operator '<op>', found ...` at the delimiter while leaving `sizeof`/`_Alignof` on their existing route-specific parsers. See `references/cust-unary-operator-missing-operand-diagnostics.md`.

- 2026-07-02: No external documentation was needed for binary-operator missing-RHS diagnostics. Root cause: the precedence-climbing binary parser levels (`||`, `&&`, bitwise operators, equality/comparison, shifts, additive, and multiplicative/remainder) consumed the operator and delegated directly to the next parser level, so malformed forms such as `return 1 + ;`, `return (1 && );`, `return 1 << ];`, and `if (1 == })` fell through to generic primary-expression messages like `expected expression, found Semi/RParen/RBracket/RBrace`. Implementation decision: preserve the consumed `LocatedToken` for each binary operator, share a narrow post-operator delimiter/terminator check, and report `expected expression after binary operator '<op>', found ...` at the delimiter while preserving valid precedence parsing.

- 2026-07-02: No external documentation was needed for assignment-operator missing-RHS diagnostics. Root cause: both `parse_assignment_expr()` and statement-oriented assignment parsers delegated directly to expression parsing after consuming `=`, `+=`, `<<=`, and other assignment operators, so malformed forms such as `value = ;`, `value += )`, and `value <<= }` fell through to generic primary-expression messages like `expected expression, found Semi/RParen/RBrace`. Implementation decision: share a narrow post-operator delimiter/terminator check across expression assignments and scalar/deref/array/struct-field assignment statement routes, reporting `expected expression after assignment operator '<op>', found ...` at the delimiter while preserving existing missing-operator diagnostics and valid assignment parsing.

- 2026-07-02: No external documentation was needed for conditional-operator missing-operand diagnostics. Root cause: `parse_conditional_expr()` delegated directly to expression parsing after consuming `?` and after consuming `:`, so malformed forms such as `1 ? : 2`, `1 ? 2 : ;`, and `1 ? 2 : )` fell through to generic primary-expression messages like `expected expression, found Colon/Semi/RParen`. Implementation decision: detect delimiter/terminator tokens immediately after `?` or `:` in conditional-expression parsing and report `expected expression after '?' in conditional operator, found ...` / `expected expression after ':' in conditional operator, found ...` at the offending token while preserving valid conditional expressions and the established missing-colon diagnostic.

- 2026-07-02: No external documentation was needed for comma-operator missing-RHS diagnostics. Root cause: `parse_comma_expr()` delegated directly to assignment-expression parsing after consuming `,`, so malformed forms such as `(x = 1,)`, `(1,]`, or `return 1,;` fell through to generic primary-expression messages like `expected expression, found RParen`. Implementation decision: detect delimiter/terminator tokens immediately after a comma operator and report `expected expression after comma operator, found ...` at that token while preserving valid comma-expression parsing and call/declaration-list comma separator routes.

- 2026-07-02: No external documentation was needed for unmatched top-level closing-delimiter diagnostics. Root cause: `parse_program()` already special-cased unmatched top-level `}` but let file-scope `)` and `]` fall through to the generic `unexpected token at top level: RParen/RBracket` branch. Implementation decision: mirror statement-level delimiter diagnostics at the top-level parse boundary for `Token::RParen` and `Token::RBracket`, preserving exact source locations and the existing `}` handling.

- 2026-07-02: No external documentation was needed for statement-only control-flow diagnostics inside `for` clauses. Root cause: `parse_for()` only special-cased `break` and `continue` before falling through to generic `unexpected token in for initializer/increment: ...`, so malformed `for (return 1; ; )`, `for (if (...) ...; ; )`, and similar statement-only forms had weaker diagnostics than the recently completed top-level/function-body control-flow checks. Implementation decision: add a narrow `statement_only_control_flow_error(...)` classifier for `for` initializer/increment parse routes, preserving `else without matching if`, `case/default label outside switch`, and valid `for` loop behavior while reporting context-specific messages for statement keywords.

- 2026-07-01: No external documentation was needed for stray/top-level control-flow diagnostics. Root cause: after adding top-level `return`/`break`/`continue` diagnostics, other statement-only control-flow keywords (`if`, `else`, `while`, `do`, `for`, `switch`, `case`, `default`) still fell through `parse_program()` to generic `unexpected token at top level`, and unmatched `else` inside function bodies fell through to generic statement parsing. Implementation decision: reject statement-only control-flow keywords explicitly in `parse_program()` with route-specific messages and add a `Token::Else` branch in `parse_stmt()` for `else without matching if`, preserving valid `if`/dangling-else, loop, and switch parsing.

- 2026-07-01: No external documentation was needed for top-level unsupported control-flow diagnostics. Root cause: file-scope `return`, `break`, and `continue` tokens fell through `parse_program()` to the generic top-level unexpected-token branch even though equivalent function-body/runtime escape paths had targeted messages. Implementation decision: reject those keywords explicitly in `parse_program()` with `Parser::error_at(...)`, preserving exact source locations and not changing valid loop/switch/function return behavior.

- 2026-07-01: No external documentation was needed for unhandled `break`/`continue` source-location diagnostics. Root cause: the parser had token locations for `break;` and `continue;`, but `Stmt::Break`/`Stmt::Continue` and `ExecFlow` discarded that metadata before top-level/function/`for`-clause escape handling reported `break outside loop` / `continue outside loop`. Implementation decision: carry `LocatedToken` through the AST and execution flow, report `break outside loop or switch at line ..., column ...` and `continue outside loop at line ..., column ...`, and keep valid loop/switch consumption matching existing behavior.

- 2026-07-01: No external documentation was needed for unsupported complex type diagnostics. Root cause: `_Complex` and `_Imaginary` lexed as ordinary identifiers, so unsupported C complex/imaginary declarations/type queries/casts fell through to generic top-level, identifier, or grouped-expression diagnostics. Implementation decision: lex `_Complex`/`_Imaginary` as dedicated tokens, route declaration/type-name/cast contexts to `parse_decl_type_with_embedded_qualifiers(...)`, and reject them with `complex types are not supported` at the keyword. See `references/cust-complex-type-diagnostics.md`.

- 2026-07-01: No external documentation was needed for unsupported floating-point type diagnostics. Root cause: `float` and `double` lexed as ordinary identifiers, so unsupported C floating-point declarations/type queries/casts fell through to generic `unexpected token`, identifier, or grouped-expression diagnostics before the parser could explain that Cust has no floating-point runtime model. Implementation decision: lex `float`/`double` as dedicated tokens, route declaration/type-name/cast contexts to `parse_decl_type_with_embedded_qualifiers(...)`, and reject them with `floating-point types are not supported` at the keyword. See `references/cust-floating-point-type-diagnostics.md`.

- 2026-07-01: No external documentation was needed for aggregate field-assignment result field access. Root cause: `parse_postfix_suffix()` classified aggregate assignment result targets such as variables, array elements, and pointer dereference assignments as valid `.` operands, but omitted direct and struct-pointer aggregate field assignments (`Expr::StructSet` / `Expr::StructPtrSet`), so `(line.start = replacement).x` failed with `invalid struct field access target`. Implementation decision: classify those assignment expressions as aggregate-valued postfix targets, recover their result type from aggregate field metadata for non-evaluating `sizeof`, and add runtime aggregate-field assignment helpers that deep-copy same-type `StructFieldValue::Struct` RHS values into direct and pointer-selected fields. See `references/cust-aggregate-field-assignment-result-field-access.md`.
- 2026-07-01: No external documentation was needed for aggregate element-assignment result field access. Root cause: `parse_postfix_suffix()` allowed `.` after aggregate-valued variables, calls, conditionals, comma expressions, direct assignments, and pointer dereference assignments, but not after root aggregate-array element assignments (`Expr::ArraySet`) or embedded aggregate-array element assignments (`Expr::StructArraySet`), so `(points[0] = replacement).x` failed with `invalid struct field access target`. Implementation decision: classify aggregate element assignment results as valid aggregate `.` targets and teach `aggregate_expr_type_name()` to recover their by-value result type, using `direct_struct_aggregate_array_field_type(...)` for embedded aggregate-array fields. See `references/cust-aggregate-element-assignment-field-access.md`.
- 2026-07-01: No external documentation was needed for `sizeof` over scalar fields selected from embedded aggregate-array elements. Root cause: `sizeof_expr()` returned `INT_SIZE` for all `Expr::StructFieldArrayElementGet`/set/compound-set variants, so `sizeof(line.points[0].tag)` treated a selected `char` field as Cust `int`. Implementation decision: resolve the embedded `StructFieldValue::StructArray` element type from metadata and then route the selected field path through `sizeof_aggregate_field_type(...)`, preserving non-evaluation of indexes, assignment RHS values, and comma-left side effects. See `references/cust-sizeof-embedded-aggregate-array-element-fields.md`.
- 2026-07-01: No external documentation was needed for `sizeof` over aggregate element assignment expressions. Root cause: `sizeof_expr()` routed `Expr::ArraySet { name, .. }` through `sizeof_indexed_value()`, which rejected `Value::StructArray`, and routed direct embedded aggregate-array element assignment through a scalar array-field helper that rejected `StructFieldValue::StructArray`. Implementation decision: return the deterministic aggregate element size for root struct arrays and embedded aggregate-array fields while preserving non-evaluation of element assignments and side-effecting comma-left operands. See `references/cust-sizeof-aggregate-element-assignment-expressions.md`.
- 2026-06-30: No external documentation was needed for `sizeof` over aggregate assignment expressions.
