- 2026-06-25: No external documentation was needed for inline enum definitions in function parameter type specifiers. Root cause: `parse_params()` parsed inline enum specifiers and queued their constants, but `parse_function_declaration()` cleared that pending state after parameter parsing to prevent accidental file-scope leakage, so function-body references such as `return MODE_READY;` failed with `undefined variable 'MODE_READY'`. Implementation decision: take pending parameter enum constants after `parse_params()` and insert the resulting `EnumDecl` as the first statement of function definitions only; discard them for prototypes. Native compiler-oracle coverage is skipped because `cc -std=c11 -Wall -Wextra -Werror` warns that enum tags declared inside parameter lists will not be visible outside the declaration. See `references/cust-inline-enum-parameter-definitions.md`.

- 2026-06-25: No external documentation was needed for inline enum definitions in top-level function return type specifiers. Root cause: function-definition lookahead only skipped the already-declared `enum Tag` type-name shape and did not skip `enum Tag { ... }` bodies, so `enum Status { READY = 5 } choose(void);` fell into variable declaration parsing and reported `expected '=' after variable declaration, found LParen`. Implementation decision: add an enum-type-specifier lookahead skipper for `starts_function_definition()`, emit pending return-type inline enum constants as file-scope `EnumDecl`s before registering the function/prototype, and clear pending constants after parameter parsing to avoid leaking parameter-list inline enum constants. See `references/cust-inline-enum-return-types.md`.

- 2026-06-25: No external documentation was needed for direct named-enum compound literals. Root cause: direct `enum Tag` type names already lowered to `DeclType::Scalar(CType::Int)` in `parse_decl_type("cast type")`, but `starts_cast_type_after_lparen()` omitted `Token::Enum`, so `(enum State){READY}` was parsed as a grouped expression and failed with `expected expression, found Enum` before the cast parser could run. Implementation decision: add `Token::Enum` to cast lookahead and rely on existing scalar/array compound-literal lowering; native oracle coverage uses an enum pointer initialized from `(enum State[3]){...}` rather than invalid array assignment from a compound literal. See `references/cust-enum-compound-literals.md`.

- 2026-06-25: No external documentation was needed for scalar array-field pointer-expression decay. Root cause: `eval_pointer()` already knew how to decay direct, struct-array element, and struct-pointer scalar array fields, but `expr_is_pointer_value()` classified only variable arrays and pointer fields as pointer-valued for relational/additive expressions, and `eval_truthy()` still routed field arrays through scalar evaluation. Implementation decision: add decay-aware pointer-expression classification for `StructGet`, `StructElementGet`, and `StructPtrGet` when the final field is a pointer, scalar array, or aggregate array; keep the old pointer-field-only helper for pointer-field assignment routing; and include field-element address expressions in pointer classification. Native oracle fixtures avoid tautological pointer self-comparison and direct array-address truthiness warnings under `-Wall -Wextra -Werror`. See `references/cust-scalar-array-field-pointer-expressions.md`.

- 2026-06-24: No external documentation was needed for unsupported parenthesized pointer typedef declarator diagnostics. Root cause: after parenthesized ordinary typedef declarator names were accepted, `parse_typedef_declarator()` still classified every `(` `*` alias form as a function-pointer typedef alias, so pointer-to-array-style forms such as `typedef int (*Row)[3];` reported the wrong unsupported feature. Implementation decision: reuse `parenthesized_pointer_declarator_is_function_at(...)` in the typedef path to preserve `function pointer typedef aliases are not supported` for `(*name)(...)` while reporting `parenthesized pointer typedef aliases are not supported` for other parenthesized pointer typedef declarators. No pointer-to-array typedef runtime support was added.

- 2026-06-24: No external documentation was needed for parenthesized typedef declarator names. Root cause: after ordinary declarations learned to accept `(name)`, `parse_typedef_declarator()` still called `expect_ident_after(...)` directly after the optional `*`/post-star qualifiers, so ordinary C alias forms such as `typedef int (Count);`, `typedef int *(IntPtr);`, and `typedef int (Scores)[3];` failed at `LParen`. Implementation decision: reuse the existing narrow `parse_declarator_name(...)` helper in typedef parsing so supported alias forms accept optional parentheses, while the early `(` `*` guard continues to preserve targeted function-pointer typedef diagnostics. No new function-type or pointer-to-array alias support was added.

- 2026-06-24: No external documentation was needed for parenthesized ordinary declarator names. Root cause: after the previous run taught function declarators to accept `(name)`, ordinary declaration parsers still called `expect_ident_after(...)` directly in parameter, variable, aggregate-variable, declaration-list, and aggregate-field declarator positions, so warning-free C forms such as `int (value)`, `int *(cursor)`, `int (items)[3]`, and `struct Point (point)` failed at `LParen`. Implementation decision: add a shared parser helper that accepts `(` `Ident` `)` as a declarator name in supported non-`(*name)` positions, reuse it across scalar/pointer/array/aggregate declarations and fields, and leave existing parenthesized pointer/function-pointer diagnostics in place for `(*name)` forms.

- 2026-06-24: No external documentation was needed for unsupported direct array-return declarator diagnostics. Root cause: `parse_function_declaration()` consumed the function name and parameter list for `int make(void)[2]` before checking only for `;` or `{`, so the unsupported array-return suffix fell into the generic `expected '{' after function header, found LBracket` diagnostic. Implementation decision: after `expect_closing_paren_after("function parameters")`, detect `Token::LBracket` before prototype/body parsing and report `array return types are not supported` at the suffix. See `references/cust-array-return-type-diagnostics.md`.

- 2026-06-24: No external documentation was needed for parenthesized function declarator names. Root cause: `starts_function_definition()` and `parse_function_declaration()` only recognized the direct `Ident (` declarator shape after supported return-type specifiers, so ordinary C declarations like `int (add)(int, int);` and `int (main)(void)` failed with `expected function name after return type, found LParen`. Implementation decision: accept `( Ident ) (` in function-definition/prototype lookahead and parse the optional parenthesized name with a shared helper used by top-level functions and block-scope prototypes, while leaving the existing `(` followed by `*` function-pointer diagnostic intact. See `references/cust-parenthesized-function-declarators.md`.

- 2026-06-24: No external documentation was needed for const array typedef compound literals. Root cause: after array typedef compound literal support, `Expr::ArrayLiteral` / `Expr::AggregateArrayLiteral` carried length and initializer metadata but not top-level read-only metadata from explicit `const` type names or const-qualified array typedef aliases, so `typedef const int Scores[2]; int *p = (Scores){1, 2};` incorrectly passed pointer conversion. Implementation decision: add `read_only` metadata to scalar and aggregate array compound-literal expressions, compute it in `parse_cast()` from the leading `const` plus `type_alias_is_const(...)`, feed it into evaluated `ArrayValue` / `StructArray` storage, and include it in `pointer_expr_points_to_const()` so mutable pointer flows reject const array literals before evaluation. See `references/cust-const-array-typedef-compound-literals.md`.

- 2026-06-24: No external documentation was needed for array typedef compound literals. Root cause: `parse_decl_type("cast type")` already resolved one-dimensional array typedef aliases as `DeclType::Array`, but `parse_cast()` treated that variant only as an unsupported cast and emitted `pointer casts are not supported` before checking for a following compound-literal initializer. Implementation decision: when `DeclType::Array(PointeeType, len)` is followed by `{`, lower scalar/char aliases to `Expr::ArrayLiteral { len: Some(len), ... }` and aggregate aliases to `Expr::AggregateArrayLiteral { len: Some(len), ... }`; when no initializer follows, preserve the existing unsupported cast diagnostic. See `references/cust-array-typedef-compound-literals.md`.

- 2026-06-24: No external documentation was needed for unsupported parenthesized pointer aggregate field diagnostics. Root cause: `parse_aggregate_definition_body()` checked for `*`-prefixed pointer fields and bit-field `:` suffixes, but when a field declarator started with `(` followed by `*`, it fell through to `expect_ident_after("struct field name after type")` and reported a generic missing-name diagnostic. Implementation decision: mirror the ordinary declaration/parameter lookahead before aggregate field-name parsing; if `parenthesized_pointer_declarator_is_function_at(self.pos)` is true, report `function pointer aggregate fields are not supported`, otherwise report `parenthesized pointer aggregate fields are not supported`. No function-pointer or pointer-to-array field runtime support was added.

- 2026-06-24: No external documentation was needed for nested named aggregate field definitions. Root cause: `parse_aggregate_definition_body()` only recursed for anonymous `struct { ... }` / `union { ... }` field definitions; field-local named definitions such as `struct Point { int x; int y; } origin;` fell into the prior-tag lookup path and failed with `undefined struct type 'Point'`. Implementation decision: treat `struct`/`union` followed by either `{` or `Ident {` as an inline aggregate definition in field declaration specifiers, recurse through `parse_aggregate_definition_body(false, true)`, and then reuse the existing field declarator-list loop so later fields can refer to the newly installed tag metadata.

- 2026-06-23: No external documentation was needed for const embedded aggregate-array element copy assignment through struct pointers. Root cause: `slot->points[0] = replacement` lowers to aggregate pointer assignment through `AddressOfStructPtrArrayField`, but `pointer_expr_points_to_const()` only inherited constness from the struct pointer expression and missed the selected field's `const struct Point points[2];` metadata, so the write mutated a const aggregate-array field. Implementation decision: extend metadata-only const tracing for `StructPtrArrayGet` / `AddressOfStructPtrArrayField` by reading the struct pointer's pointee type and walking the selected field path with `const_aggregate_field_label_for_path(...)`, preserving the existing direct variable-backed protection and returning the parent-field diagnostic `cannot assign to const struct field 'points'`.

- 2026-06-23: No external documentation was needed for const aggregate-array field diagnostics on aggregate compound literals. Root cause: `((struct Box){...}).points[1].x = 9` parses as a `StructPtrSet` over pointer arithmetic whose base is `AggregateFieldGet(fields=["points"])`; the existing `eval_aggregate_literal_field_pointer()` path materialized the const field as a hidden read-only struct array, so writes were safely rejected but with the generic `cannot assign through pointer to const` diagnostic. Implementation decision: keep `ensure_pointer_expr_pointee_mutable()` as the central safety gate, but add metadata-only tracing over `AggregateFieldGet` and pointer `+`/`-` expressions to recover the originating const aggregate field label and report `cannot assign to const struct field 'points'` without evaluating compound-literal initializers. See `references/cust-const-aggregate-array-field-compound-literal-writes.md`.

- 2026-06-23: No external documentation was needed for const aggregate field nested-write enforcement. Root cause: `assign_scalar_field_in_map()` checked `StructFieldValue::is_const()` only at the final path component, so nested writes through a const aggregate field (`box.point.x = 3`) bypassed the parent field's read-only metadata. Implementation decision: check parent field constness before recursing into nested `StructFieldValue::Struct`, reporting `cannot assign to const struct field 'point'` for named and anonymous aggregate fields while preserving initializer/read behavior. See `references/cust-const-aggregate-field-nested-writes.md`.

- 2026-06-23: No external documentation was needed for anonymous aggregate fields. Implementation decision: inside `parse_aggregate_definition_body()`, detect field declaration specifiers that start with `struct`/`union` followed by `{`, call `parse_aggregate_definition_body(false, true)` recursively, and lower the unique internal type name to `DeclType::Struct(...)` so existing field declarator-list, initializer, nested field access, array field, and copy semantics handle the runtime behavior. Separately spelled anonymous aggregate pointer types remain distinct and incompatible. See `references/cust-anonymous-aggregate-fields.md`.

- 2026-06-23: No external documentation was needed for anonymous aggregate pointer casts. Implementation decision: `parse_cast()` must detect `struct`/`union` followed by `{ ... }` and then a `*` before the anonymous aggregate array/compound-literal branches, lower the spelling to `Expr::PointerCast` with `PointeeType::Struct(type_name)`, preserve leading `const` as pointee-const metadata, and avoid installing source-level tags or typedef aliases. Fixtures should use null comparisons and non-evaluating `sizeof(*(anonymous *)0)` because separately spelled anonymous aggregate types are distinct. See `references/cust-anonymous-aggregate-pointer-casts.md`.

- 2026-06-23: No external documentation was needed for anonymous aggregate compound literals. Implementation decision: `parse_cast()` must detect `struct`/`union` followed by `{` before the ordinary named-tag `parse_decl_type(...)` path, reuse `parse_aggregate_definition_body(false, true)` to create a unique internal anonymous aggregate type identity, then continue through the existing aggregate or aggregate-array compound-literal initializer paths. Do not install a source-level tag or typedef alias for these expression-local types; compiler-oracle coverage should use direct field/index behavior rather than assigning anonymous aggregate pointers across separate anonymous type spellings. See `references/cust-anonymous-aggregate-compound-literals.md`.

- 2026-06-23: No external documentation was needed for anonymous aggregate `sizeof` / `_Alignof` type-name operands. Implementation decision: `parse_sizeof_like_type_name()` must detect `struct`/`union` followed by `{` before the named-tag `parse_decl_type(...)` path, reuse `parse_aggregate_definition_body(false, true)` for field parsing, and store a cloned `StructTypeDef` in `SizeOfType::AnonymousAggregate` for direct size/alignment queries. Pointer and one-dimensional array suffixes continue through existing pointer/array type-query metadata. Native oracle coverage must use ABI-independent relationships because Cust deliberately omits native struct padding. See `references/cust-sizeof-alignof-anonymous-aggregate-type-names.md`.

- 2026-06-23: No external documentation was needed for anonymous aggregate parenthesized pointer declarator diagnostics. Implementation decision: `parse_aggregate_var_decl_after_type()` must mirror the generic declaration parser's early `(` followed by `*` lookahead before expecting the aggregate variable name, so unsupported forms like `struct { int x; } (*slot);` report `parenthesized pointer declarations are not supported` at the `(` instead of a misleading missing-name diagnostic. Function-shaped parenthesized declarators reuse the existing function-pointer diagnostic branch. See `references/cust-anonymous-aggregate-parenthesized-pointer-declarator-diagnostics.md`.

- 2026-06-23: No external documentation was needed for storage/alignment anonymous aggregate declaration-context routing. Implementation decision: after consuming local `static`, `_Thread_local`, `auto`/`register`, or `_Alignas` metadata, parser helpers must check `starts_qualified_aggregate_declaration()` before the scalar declaration branch so `const struct { ... }` / `volatile union { ... }` reuse `parse_aggregate_var_decl()`. For static locals, wrap the resulting `Stmt::Many`/aggregate declarations through `wrap_static_local_declarations()` so enum declarations remain unwrapped while actual anonymous aggregate variables get persistent static ids.

- 2026-06-23: No external documentation was needed for qualified anonymous aggregate `for` initializer declarations. Implementation decision: `parse_for` must check `starts_qualified_aggregate_declaration()` before routing `const`/`volatile`/bare `_Atomic` starts into the scalar declaration parser; otherwise `const struct { ... } cfg` falls through to named-struct parsing and fails at the anonymous body. The fix routes qualifier-leading and bare `struct`/`union` initializer declarations through `parse_aggregate_var_decl()`, preserving unique anonymous type identity, same-declaration pointer declarators, and const pointer-view diagnostics. See `references/cust-qualified-anonymous-aggregate-for-initializers.md`.

- 2026-06-23: No external documentation was needed for anonymous aggregate `for` initializer declarations. Implementation decision: ordinary `for` declaration initializers should reuse the same aggregate declaration parser as block statements; `parse_for` now routes plain `struct`/`union` starts to `parse_aggregate_var_decl()` so anonymous aggregate initializer declarations consume the `for` initializer semicolon and preserve unique anonymous type identity, field access, and loop scoping. See `references/cust-anonymous-aggregate-for-initializers.md`.

- 2026-06-22: No external documentation was needed for const anonymous aggregate array negative coverage. Implementation decision: no parser/runtime change was required because same-declaration anonymous aggregate pointer declarators already preserve leading `const` metadata; the new invalid fixture targets the pointer-view path (`const struct { ... } points[2], *slot = points + 1; slot->x = 9;`) and expects `cannot assign through pointer to const`. A first direct `points[1].x = 9` fixture correctly produced the root-binding diagnostic `cannot assign to const variable 'points'`, so the fixture was narrowed before GREEN.

- 2026-06-22: No external documentation was needed for unsupported anonymous aggregate return-type diagnostics. Implementation decision: Cust continues to require aggregate function return types to use named tags or typedef-backed aggregate aliases; direct anonymous `struct { ... } make(...)` / `union { ... } pick(...)` return spellings now fail early with `anonymous aggregate return types are not supported` at the aggregate keyword rather than falling through to aggregate variable-declaration parsing. See `references/cust-anonymous-aggregate-return-diagnostics.md`.

- 2026-06-22: No external documentation was needed for unsupported anonymous aggregate parameter diagnostics. Implementation decision: Cust continues to require aggregate parameters to use named tags or typedef-backed aggregate aliases; direct anonymous `struct { ... }` / `union { ... }` parameter types now fail early in `parse_params` with `anonymous aggregate parameters are not supported` at the aggregate keyword rather than falling through to a generic `LBrace` type error. See `references/cust-anonymous-aggregate-parameter-diagnostics.md`.

- 2026-06-22: No external documentation was needed for unsupported enum forward-declaration diagnostics. Implementation decision: Cust still does not support incomplete enum declarations such as `enum Color;`, but `parse_decl_type` now detects a semicolon immediately after the enum tag and reports `forward enum declarations are not supported` at the semicolon before the generic undefined-enum-type path runs.

- 2026-06-22: No external documentation was needed for the mixed declaration-context conformance fixture. Implementation decision: add a warning-free native C compiler-oracle fixture that deliberately combines already-supported surfaces—comma-separated typedef alias declarators, inline enum object declarations, pointer-first anonymous aggregate declaration lists, const anonymous aggregate arrays and pointer views, aggregate compound literal returns, and typedef-spelled aggregate return values—so future parser/runtime changes are checked against an integrated ordinary-C program. The focused interpreter test passed immediately, so no production code was changed.

- 2026-06-22: No external documentation was needed for inline enum object declarations in storage/`for` contexts. Implementation decision: allow `enum` through declaration-context routing for `for` initializer, local `static`, `auto`, `register`, `_Thread_local`, and `_Alignas` paths, then preserve inline enum constants as a preceding runtime `EnumDecl`. Static-local wrapping must recurse through `Stmt::Many` and leave `EnumDecl` unwrapped while assigning static-local ids only to actual variable declarations; otherwise inline enum constants would either be rejected after `static` or incorrectly treated as static variables.

- 2026-06-22: No external documentation was needed for comma-separated typedef alias declarator lists. Implementation decision: parse shared typedef declaration specifiers into a base `DeclType`, then parse each alias declarator with its own `*`, post-star qualifiers, and `[N]` suffix before registering parser-only alias metadata. This preserves `typedef const int *View, Table[2];` as pointer-to-const plus const array alias, and `typedef int *Ptr, * const Slot;` as ordinary pointer alias plus const pointer-slot alias. See `references/cust-comma-separated-typedef-aliases.md`.

- 2026-06-21: No external documentation was needed for same-declaration anonymous aggregate array plus pointer declarator conformance coverage. Implementation decision: existing anonymous aggregate declaration-list parsing already preserves a single unique internal anonymous type across array and pointer declarators, so this run added interpreter and native C compiler-oracle fixtures for `struct { ... } points[3] = {...}, *slot = points + 1;` and matching anonymous union arrays/pointers rather than changing runtime code. Focused RED initially exposed an incorrect expected test total; corrected fixture arithmetic to 31 before GREEN verification.

- 2026-06-21: No external documentation was needed for qualified anonymous aggregate object declarations and same-declaration anonymous aggregate pointer declarator lists. Implementation decision: route qualifier-leading aggregate declarations such as `const struct { ... } value` through `parse_aggregate_var_decl` so anonymous aggregate definitions can still allocate a unique internal type identity, then reuse the existing declaration-list tail to keep `value`, same-type copies, and `*slot = &value` pointer declarators on the same anonymous type. Standalone anonymous aggregate parameter types remain outside the supported subset because they create unnameable distinct C types.

- 2026-06-21: No external documentation was needed for anonymous aggregate object declarations. Implementation decision: reuse the same unique internal aggregate type identity path that backs anonymous aggregate typedef definitions, but do not install a user-visible tag or typedef alias for `struct { ... } object` / `union { ... } object` declarations. The parser now handles the inline aggregate body before the ordinary aggregate declarator tail, so supported global/local variables and arrays reuse existing aggregate initializer/runtime paths and C compiler-oracle coverage.

- 2026-06-21: No external documentation was needed for unsupported aggregate bit-field diagnostics. Implementation decision: Cust still does not support C bit-field storage/layout semantics, but `:` after a parsed struct/union field declarator should be detected before the generic field semicolon helper so users get targeted `bit-field aggregate fields are not supported` diagnostics for both `struct` and `union` fields. See `references/cust-aggregate-bit-field-diagnostics.md`.

- 2026-06-21: No external documentation was needed for unsupported aggregate forward-declaration diagnostics. Implementation decision: Cust still does not support incomplete aggregate types, but top-level `struct Name;` and `union Name;` should be detected before aggregate variable-declaration parsing so users get targeted `forward struct/union declarations are not supported` diagnostics rather than misleading `undefined struct type` errors. See `references/cust-aggregate-forward-declaration-diagnostics.md`.

- 2026-06-21: No external documentation was needed for typedef-backed aggregate field declaration lists. Implementation decision: when parsing aggregate field specifiers, preserve alias-carried const metadata by checking `type_alias_is_const(...)` before `parse_decl_type(...)` consumes a typedef name. This keeps `typedef int * const ConstIntSlot; struct Cursor { ConstIntSlot fixed, backup; };` as const pointer-slot fields and preserves existing `typedef const int *ConstIntView;` pointee-const metadata. See `references/cust-aggregate-field-typedef-const-metadata.md`.

- 2026-06-21: No external documentation was needed for comma-separated aggregate field declaration lists; this is ordinary C declarator-list syntax applied inside `struct`/`union` definitions. Implementation decision: parse the shared declaration specifiers once and then parse each field declarator's own `*`/qualifier and `[N]` suffix before the terminating semicolon, so forms like `int *head, *tail;` and `int weights[2], offsets[2];` preserve per-field metadata while duplicate-field diagnostics still point at the duplicate declarator.

- 2026-06-21: No external documentation was needed for aggregate pointer declaration-list conformance coverage. Implementation decision: this run added explicit Cust and C compiler-oracle fixtures for already-supported ordinary C declarator-list forms `struct Point *p = points, *q = points + 1;` and `union Number *n = numbers, *m = numbers + 2;`; the focused interpreter test passed immediately, so no production parser/runtime code was changed.

- 2026-06-21: No external documentation was needed for embedded aggregate-array element copy assignment. Implementation decision: direct `line.points[i] = aggregate` syntax reaches `Expr::StructArraySet`, which previously only routed scalar array fields; direct embedded aggregate-array fields now dispatch to a new aggregate assignment helper using `StructFieldValue::StructArray` storage and existing deep-copy semantics. Struct-pointer forms such as `slot->points[i] = aggregate` reuse `AddressOfStructPtrArrayField` and the existing aggregate pointer assignment path once discard-context detection checks the evaluated pointer target instead of only variable-shaped pointers.
- 2026-06-21: No external documentation was needed for direct aggregate-array element copy assignment. Implementation decision: `points[i] = aggregate` already parses as `Stmt::ArrayAssign` for statement syntax and `Expr::ArraySet` for assignment-expression syntax, so the runtime now dispatches those nodes by LHS storage type. When the LHS is a supported aggregate array or aggregate pointer, Cust deep-clones the same-type RHS aggregate through `eval_struct_index_assignment_expr`; scalar arrays continue through the existing scalar assignment path. Detailed recovery notes live in `references/cust-aggregate-array-element-copy-assignment.md`.

- 2026-06-21: No external documentation was needed for comma-separated pointer/array/aggregate declaration-list parity. Implementation decision: declaration-list initializers use assignment-expression precedence rather than the full comma-expression parser so source commas remain declarator separators; explicit parenthesized comma expressions still work inside initializers. The parser now reuses `Stmt::Many` for same-scope pointer, array, struct, union, and array-alias declarators, preserving const and pointee metadata per declarator.

- 2026-06-20: No external documentation was needed for comma-separated scalar declaration lists. Implementation decision: Cust now lowers scalar declarator lists into a same-scope `Stmt::Many` wrapper rather than a block so `int a = 1, b;` and `for (int i = 0, j = 3; ...)` preserve C declaration scope and omitted-initializer zeroing. Static-local lists are expanded into separate `StaticLocal` declarations with distinct persistent storage IDs. Pointer, array, and aggregate declarator lists remain follow-up work.

- 2026-06-20: No external documentation was needed for one-level pointer cast expression support. Implementation decision: Cust now accepts `(T *)expr` and pointer-typedef cast spellings for the existing one-level scalar/aggregate pointer subset, with casts usable in pointer expression contexts and non-evaluating `sizeof(*(T *)0)` type inference. Runtime pointer values remain interpreter-owned and type-checked at assignment/argument boundaries; explicit casts do not discard const-pointee safety because cast metadata preserves source constness as well as target `const`.

- 2026-06-20: No external documentation was needed for inferred aggregate array declaration support. Implementation decision: empty-bracket aggregate array declarations are accepted only with immediate brace initializers, then lowered to the existing fixed-length `Stmt::StructArrayDecl` runtime path after inferring length from positional and designated aggregate entries; initializer-less `struct Point points[];` remains rejected because the object type is incomplete.

- 2026-05-12: No external documentation was needed for inferred scalar array declaration support. Implementation decision: empty-bracket scalar array declarations are accepted only when immediately followed by an initializer, then lowered to the existing fixed-length `Stmt::ArrayDecl` runtime path after inferring length from positional/designated/string initializers; initializer-less `int values[];` remains rejected because the object type is incomplete.

- 2026-05-12: No external documentation was needed for block-scope function prototype syntax parity. Implementation decision: local function declarations/prototypes are parsed as no-op statements that reuse Cust's existing function signature parser (including unnamed and array parameters) while runtime calls still resolve against top-level function definitions; nested block-scope function definitions remain unsupported.

- 2026-05-12: No external documentation was needed for unsupported function typedef diagnostic polish. Implementation decision: Cust still does not implement C function type aliases such as `typedef int Callback(int);`; the parser now rejects the function declarator suffix after the alias name with `function typedef aliases are not supported`, avoiding the misleading generic missing-semicolon diagnostic while leaving existing function-pointer typedef diagnostics intact.

- 2026-05-12: No external documentation was needed for unsupported C11 `_Generic` diagnostic polish. Implementation decision: Cust still does not implement generic selections/type dispatch; the lexer recognizes `_Generic` as a keyword and the parser rejects it at primary-expression position with `generic selections are not supported`, avoiding misleading association-list parser errors. See `references/cust-generic-selection-diagnostics.md`.

- 2026-05-12: No external documentation was needed for unsupported preprocessor-directive diagnostic polish. Implementation decision: Cust remains preprocessor-free; the lexer now detects `#` before the generic unexpected-character fallback and reports `preprocessor directives are not supported` with source-line/caret context, rather than adding a directive token or invoking a native preprocessor. See `references/cust-preprocessor-directive-diagnostics.md`.

- 2026-05-12: No external documentation was needed for unsupported `goto`/label diagnostic polish. Implementation decision: arbitrary jumps/labels remain outside Cust's structured-control-flow subset; the parser now recognizes `goto` as a keyword and detects statement-leading `identifier:` labels before expression-statement parsing so both unsupported forms get targeted diagnostics.

- 2026-05-12: No external documentation was needed for variadic function-parameter diagnostic polish. Implementation decision: C varargs remain outside Cust's runtime subset; the parser now detects `...` in function parameter lists before generic type parsing and reports a targeted unsupported diagnostic at the first dot.

- 2026-05-12: No external documentation was needed for permuted scalar type specifiers. Implementation decision: supported C scalar declaration specifier permutations are parsed by consuming interleaved scalar specifier tokens plus qualifiers and lowering them to Cust's deterministic scalar model; native `cc -std=c11 -Wall -Wextra -Werror` was used only as a compiler-oracle fixture check for warning-free forms. See `references/cust-permuted-scalar-type-specifiers.md`.

- 2026-05-12: No external documentation was needed for block-scope `extern` object declarations. Implementation decision: parse supported local `extern` object declarations with existing declaration parsers and lower them to `Stmt::Empty` so they do not create local storage or shadow existing globals in Cust's single-file global model; initialized block-scope extern declarations are rejected to avoid silently discarding initializer side effects. See `references/cust-extern-local-declarations.md`.

- 2026-05-12: C99 array-parameter bracket qualifiers implemented from existing project conventions rather than external docs. Implementation decision: treat `static`, `restrict`, `volatile`, and `_Atomic` inside parameter array brackets as parser metadata/no-ops over Cust's pointer-parameter decay model, while mapping bracket `const` to the existing const pointer-slot metadata so `int values[const N]` rejects reassignment of the parameter name. See `references/cust-array-parameter-qualifiers.md`.

- 2026-05-12: No external documentation was needed for C `(void)expr` casts. Implementation decision: treat `(void)` as a parser-only cast form that evaluates the operand in discard context and has no scalar value; this keeps native C warning-suppression parity for unused scalar/pointer/void-call results while preserving Cust's existing `void ... used as scalar expression` boundary for value contexts.

- 2026-05-12: Local `cc -std=c11 -Wall -Wextra -Werror` rejected comma expressions in enum initializer and `switch case` integer-constant-expression contexts (`enumerator value ... is not an integer constant` / `case label does not reduce to an integer constant`). Cust now intentionally rejects those malformed forms with a targeted parser diagnostic rather than adding comma folding to enum/switch integer constant expressions.

- 2026-05-11: No external documentation was needed for function-pointer parameter/local declarator diagnostics. Cust still intentionally excludes runtime function pointers, but the parser now distinguishes unsupported `(*name)(...)` declarators in local declarations and function parameters from pointer-to-array parenthesized pointer declarators, preserving targeted diagnostics for both unsupported families.

- 2026-05-11: No external documentation was needed for function-pointer declarator diagnostics. Cust still intentionally excludes function pointers, but parser checks now recognize `(` followed by `*` after a function return type or typedef base type and emit targeted unsupported-form diagnostics before generic missing-name helpers run.

- 2026-05-11: No external documentation was needed for expression-form `sizeof` in enum/switch integer constant expressions. Cust now parses parenthesized `sizeof(expression)` operands in the parser-only integer-constant-expression helper and folds supported expression size metadata without runtime evaluation. Coverage uses `sizeof(1 / 0) == sizeof(int)` so both Cust and the native compiler-oracle prove the operand is unevaluated while avoiding ABI-sensitive exact integer sizes.

- 2026-05-11: No external documentation was needed for scalar casts in enum/switch integer constant expressions. Cust now folds scalar type-name casts at unary precedence in the parser-only constant-expression helper, including scalar typedef aliases, while preserving its existing deterministic no-truncation scalar-cast value model. Pointer/array/aggregate casts remain unsupported in this folding path; compiler-oracle coverage uses small `int`/`char` typedef casts to avoid native `char` signedness/truncation concerns and keeps the exit total below 256.

- 2026-05-11: No external documentation was needed for `sizeof`/`_Alignof` in enum/switch integer constant expressions. Cust now folds type-name `sizeof(...)` and `_Alignof(...)` at unary precedence in the parser-only constant-expression helper, reusing deterministic `SizeOfType` size/alignment metadata. Compiler-oracle coverage uses ABI-independent `char` array and `_Alignof(char)` forms and keeps the final exit total below 256.
- 2026-05-11: No external documentation was needed for comparison/logical/conditional integer constant expressions. Cust's parser now folds relational/equality/logical and `?:` operators for enum initializers and `switch case` labels using C-style `0`/`1` truth values while keeping the helper parser-only and non-evaluating. Note for future compiler-oracle fixture work: native process exit codes truncate to 8 bits, so keep compat fixture totals below 256 or compare relationships inside the C program instead of returning large sums.
- 2026-05-11: No external documentation was needed for broader enum/switch integer constant expressions. Cust's parser now folds a deliberately scoped precedence ladder for visible enum constants and literal expressions (`()`, unary `+`/`-`/`~`/`!`, `*`/`/`/`%`, `+`/`-`, `<<`/`>>`, `&`, `^`, `|`) in enum initializer and `switch case` contexts without evaluating runtime expressions; remaining C integer-constant operators should be added only with fixtures that need them.
- 2026-05-11: No external documentation was needed for enum/switch constant-expression parity. Cust now treats enum constants as parser-known integer constants for declaration-order enum initializer references and `switch case` labels, limited to simple `+`/`-` constant expressions so parsing stays non-evaluating and independent of runtime variables.
- 2026-05-11: No external documentation was needed for C multiplicative compound assignments. Cust now treats `*=`, `/=`, and `%=` as ordinary assignment-precedence compound operators over the existing scalar lvalue families, reusing binary `/` and `%` division-by-zero diagnostics and preserving existing pointer compound-assignment rejection behavior for pointer-valued contexts.
- 2026-05-11: No external documentation was needed for C11 `_Atomic` type qualifier/specifier syntax parity. Cust treats bare `_Atomic` and `_Atomic(type-name)` as parser-level no-op metadata over the existing deterministic storage/type model; it does not implement native atomic operations, memory ordering, lock-free guarantees, or layout changes. Native compiler-oracle coverage uses warning-free ordinary loads/stores and stable `sizeof(_Atomic char) == sizeof(char)` rather than ABI-sensitive atomic integer sizes; `_Atomic` function return type coverage remains interpreter-only because native `cc -std=c11 -Wall -Wextra -Werror` reports `-Werror=ignored-qualifiers` for qualified function returns.
- 2026-05-11: No external documentation was needed for C11 `_Thread_local` storage-class syntax parity. Cust treats `_Thread_local` as parser-level no-op storage metadata for supported object declarations in its deterministic single-thread interpreter model, reusing ordinary global storage and existing persistent local `static` storage. A local `cc -std=c11 -Wall -Wextra -Werror` smoke check showed block-scope `_Thread_local int local;` without `static`/`extern` is warning-rejected as implicitly automatic, so compiler-oracle fixtures use `static _Thread_local` for block-scope declarations.
- 2026-05-11: No external documentation was needed for C11 `_Alignas` declaration specifier syntax parity. Cust treats `_Alignas(type-name)` and `_Alignas(expression)` as parser-level no-op declaration metadata because interpreter storage is deterministic and layout/field offsets are not native-ABI based. A local `cc -std=c11 -Wall -Wextra -Werror` smoke check confirmed `_Alignas(8) int x;`, `_Alignas(int)` struct fields, and block locals compile warning-free as a native compiler-oracle fixture shape.
- 2026-05-11: No external documentation was needed for C11 `_Alignof(type-name)` syntax parity. Cust implements `_Alignof` as a non-evaluating type-name query over existing parser metadata with deterministic interpreter alignments (`char`/`_Bool` = 1, `int`/integer aliases and pointers = 8, arrays use element alignment, structs/unions use max field alignment) rather than native ABI padding. Native compiler-oracle coverage intentionally checks ABI-independent alignment relationships instead of exact Cust-specific integer/pointer/aggregate values.

- 2026-05-11: No external documentation was needed for C11 `_Static_assert` syntax parity. Cust implements `_Static_assert(condition, "message");` at top level and in block scope as a parser/runtime assertion over existing scalar truthiness semantics; the condition parser intentionally uses assignment-precedence parsing so the comma remains the assertion argument separator. Top-level assertions execute through the existing globals-before-`main()` path, and false assertions report `static assertion failed: <message>`.

- 2026-05-11: No external documentation was needed for postfix/interleaved const qualifier syntax parity. Cust now consumes shared type qualifiers after supported base type spellings as well as before them, so `int const`, `unsigned const int`, `long const int`, `struct Point const`, and alias spellings such as `typedef int const Alias` reuse the same const metadata paths as leading `const`. Native compiler-oracle fixtures avoid top-level const-qualified function return spellings because the repository's `cc -std=c11 -Wall -Wextra -Werror` flags reject ignored return qualifiers.

- 2026-05-11: No external documentation was needed for const-qualified pointer typedef aliases. Cust now keeps pointer typedef alias pointee constness (`typedef const int *Alias`) in `TypeAlias::Pointer` / `DeclType::Pointer` metadata separately from top-level alias constness (`typedef int * const Alias`) in `const_type_alias_scopes`, so declarations/parameters/returns/fields can apply existing pointee-const and pointer-slot const diagnostics independently.
- 2026-05-11: No external documentation was needed for const-qualified non-pointer typedef aliases. Cust stores parser-only const typedef metadata in a lexical scope stack parallel to ordinary typedef aliases, then merges alias constness with explicit leading qualifiers in `parse_const_qualified_decl_type` so alias-spelled scalar, aggregate, parameter, and array declarations reuse existing const enforcement. Pointer typedef aliases now carry separate pointee-const metadata as of the follow-up const pointer typedef alias run.

- 2026-05-11: No external documentation was needed for C function specifier syntax parity. Cust treats top-level `inline` and `_Noreturn` as parser-level no-op metadata over existing function prototypes/definitions, preserving the current single-file function table and return-shape behavior. Native compiler-oracle coverage uses warning-free `static inline` helper definitions and an unused `_Noreturn` prototype; defining a `_Noreturn` function that returns normally is intentionally avoided because `cc -std=c11 -Wall -Wextra -Werror` rejects that fixture shape.

- 2026-05-11: No external documentation was needed for C99 `restrict` pointer qualifier syntax parity. Cust treats `restrict` as parser-level no-op syntax over the existing interpreter-owned pointer model, preserving `const` as the only qualifier with write-enforcement metadata. Native compiler-oracle coverage keeps `restrict` attached to pointer-derived declarations (`int * restrict p`, parameters, and pointer fields) and avoids non-pointer `restrict int value` forms.

- 2026-05-11: No external documentation was needed for C `volatile` type qualifier syntax parity. Cust treats `volatile` as parser-level no-op syntax over the existing deterministic interpreter model while preserving `const` as the qualifier with write-enforcement semantics. Native C compiler-oracle coverage avoids `volatile` on function return types (`-Werror=ignored-qualifiers`) and avoids assigning volatile arrays to non-volatile pointee types (`-Werror=discarded-qualifiers`).

- 2026-05-04: Autonomous research may use local manual pages (`man`) when needed for command syntax, C/POSIX/libc behavior, or when external docs are unavailable. Record concise `man <page>` findings here just like URL-based findings.

- 2026-05-04: No external documentation was needed for lexer line/column tracking; implementation used simple 1-based position accounting over Rust `char`s in `src/lib.rs`.
- 2026-05-04: `docker compose run --rm test` initially reused the existing `cust-test:v0.1` image and showed only the previous 4 integration tests. Running `docker compose build test cust` first rebuilt images, after which `docker compose run --rm test` showed all 6 tests. Next Docker ergonomics work should prevent stale-image verification.
- 2026-05-04: Docker Compose service reference documents `pull_policy: build` as forcing Compose to build the image and rebuild it if already present: https://docs.docker.com/reference/compose-file/services/#pull_policy. Applied to both `cust` and `test` services in `docker-compose.yml` so required cron verification commands rebuild from the current checkout.
- 2026-05-04: No external documentation was needed for parser location tracking; implementation carries lexer-produced 1-based line/column metadata through `LocatedToken` in `src/lib.rs` and formats parser diagnostics at the consumed or current token.
- 2026-05-04: No external documentation was needed for lexer source context snippets; implementation formats the offending source line plus a caret under the 1-based lexer column in `src/lib.rs`.
- 2026-05-04: C block-scope semantics reference: cppreference documents compound statements/block scope and that identifiers declared in a block are visible from declaration until block end: https://en.cppreference.com/w/c/language/scope. Implemented nested interpreter scopes in `src/lib.rs` with inner shadowing and nearest-scope assignment lookup.
- 2026-05-04: C logical operator semantics reference: cppreference documents `!`, `&&`, and `||` as using scalar truth values where zero is false and nonzero is true; `&&` and `||` short-circuit and return `1` or `0`: https://en.cppreference.com/w/c/language/operator_logical. Implemented this precedence/short-circuit behavior in `src/lib.rs`.
- 2026-05-04: C `for` statement reference: cppreference documents `for (init-clause; expression-2; expression-3) statement`, optional controlling expression interpreted as nonzero/zero truth, and init-clause scope lasting until loop end: https://en.cppreference.com/w/c/language/for. Implemented declaration/assignment initializers, optional condition/increment clauses, and loop-local initializer scope in `src/lib.rs`.
- 2026-05-04: C `break` and `continue` statements did not require additional external research beyond the existing C control-flow roadmap. Implemented loop-local consumption of these control-flow signals for `while` and `for`, with propagation through nested blocks/conditionals and errors at top level in `src/lib.rs`.
- 2026-05-04: Attempted to re-check cppreference statement pages (`https://en.cppreference.com/w/c/language/statements` and `/for`) for empty/expression statement details, but direct HTTP access returned 403 in the cron environment. Implementation followed existing roadmap/C semantics: a bare `;` is a no-op statement, `expr;` evaluates and discards the value, and `for` initializer/increment clauses may use expression statements in addition to existing declarations/assignments.
- 2026-05-04: No additional external documentation was needed for ordinary integer function definitions/calls. Implementation follows the existing C-subset shape: every function has return type `int`, calls evaluate integer arguments before entering a per-call parameter scope, undefined calls and argument-count mismatches are interpreter errors, and a 1,000-call-depth limit guards runaway recursion in `src/lib.rs`.
- 2026-05-04: No additional external documentation was needed for recursive calls. Tightened the existing recursive call guard from 1,000 to 256 active calls because the new depth-limit regression exposed Rust test-process stack overflow before the old limit could return a Cust error; `src/lib.rs` now checks before entering the next function call and reports the callee name.
- 2026-05-04: No external documentation was needed for parser separator diagnostics. Implementation remains within Cust's existing parser design and adds local checks after each parsed function parameter/call argument so malformed lists report the missing separator at the unexpected token instead of falling through to generic `expected RParen`/expression errors.
- 2026-05-04: No additional external documentation was needed for first-pass `char` support. Implementation treats `char` declarations and parameters as integer-valued storage in Cust's existing `i64` interpreter model and lexes ordinary/escaped character literals into numeric codepoint values while preserving lexer line/caret diagnostics for malformed literals.
- 2026-05-04: No additional external documentation was needed for one-dimensional array support. Implementation uses zero-initialized `int`/`char` arrays backed by shared interpreter storage (`Rc<RefCell<ArrayValue>>`), supports indexed reads/writes and array parameters with declared length checks, and reports runtime negative/out-of-bounds accesses from `src/lib.rs`.
- 2026-05-04: Attempted to check cppreference string literal semantics at https://en.cppreference.com/w/c/language/string_literal, but direct HTTP access returned 403 in the cron environment. Implementation follows the C-subset roadmap and standard C behavior needed here: ordinary string literals are NUL-terminated byte arrays, support the same simple escapes as character literals plus `\"`, can be indexed and passed to `char` array parameters, and are read-only in Cust's interpreter storage model.
- 2026-05-04: No external documentation was needed for targeted parser missing-semicolon diagnostics. Implementation uses the parser's existing `LocatedToken` line/column data and adds context-specific semicolon expectations after declarations, assignments, expression statements, and return statements in `src/lib.rs`.
- 2026-05-04: No external documentation was needed for targeted parser missing-`]` diagnostics. Implementation uses the parser's existing `LocatedToken` line/column data and adds context-specific bracket expectations after array declaration lengths, array parameter lengths, indexed array assignments/reads, and string-literal indexing in `src/lib.rs`.
- 2026-05-04: No external documentation was needed for targeted parser missing-`)` diagnostics. Implementation uses the parser's existing `LocatedToken` line/column data and adds context-specific parenthesis expectations after grouped expressions, function call arguments, function definition parameters, and `if`/`while`/`for` headers in `src/lib.rs`.
- 2026-05-04: No external documentation was needed for targeted parser missing-`{` diagnostics. Implementation uses the parser's existing `LocatedToken` line/column data and adds context-specific opening-brace expectations after function headers and `if`/`else`/`while`/`for` control-flow headers in `src/lib.rs`.
- 2026-05-04: No external documentation was needed for additional parser delimiter diagnostics. Implementation adds a shared `expect_opening_paren_after()` helper for function/control-flow headers, reuses `expect_semicolon_after()` for `break`, `continue`, and `for` condition clauses, reports unmatched stray closing delimiters, and rejects `break`/`continue` in non-body `for` clauses in `src/lib.rs`.
- 2026-05-05: User clarified native compilers are allowed and recommended only as test oracles: tests may compile supported fixture programs with GCC/Clang/cc, run the native result, and compare against Cust interpreted output. They must not be used as Cust's implementation mechanism, runtime path, or a shortcut for deciding how to implement features. `clangd` remains editor/LSP-only and not part of verification.
- 2026-05-04: No external documentation was needed for the safe Cust pointer model design. The design intentionally avoids host-memory addresses and specifies interpreter-owned pointer targets, null/out-of-scope diagnostics, array/string decay, and concrete acceptance fixtures in `docs/plans/pointer-model.md`.
- 2026-05-04: No external documentation was needed for the `cust --version` CLI flag; it prints Cargo's compile-time `CARGO_PKG_VERSION` value from `src/main.rs` and is covered by `tests/cli.rs`.
- 2026-05-04: No external documentation was needed for the first scalar pointer milestone. Implementation follows `docs/plans/pointer-model.md` by representing scalar pointers as interpreter-owned `{scope_id, name}` targets rather than host addresses, with `0` as null and live-scope tracking for out-of-scope dereference diagnostics in `src/lib.rs`.
- 2026-05-05: No additional external documentation was needed for pointer parameters and array/string decay. Implementation follows `docs/plans/pointer-model.md` by representing array-base pointers as shared `Rc<RefCell<ArrayValue>>` storage, decaying array variables and string literals at pointer-argument/pointer-initializer boundaries, and preserving read-only string diagnostics in `src/lib.rs`.
- 2026-05-05: During full-suite verification after adding pointer array-base support, the existing recursive-call depth regression overflowed the Rust test process stack before the 256-depth guard returned a Cust error. Reproduced with `cargo test --test interpreter reports_function_name_when_recursive_calls_exceed_depth_limit -- --nocapture`; root cause was increased interpreter call-frame size after pointer-array support. Tightened `MAX_CALL_DEPTH` to 64 in `src/lib.rs`, and the existing exact diagnostic regression passed again.
- 2026-05-05: No additional external documentation was needed for `&array[index]` array-element pointers. Implementation follows `docs/plans/pointer-model.md` by storing interpreter-owned array-element targets as shared array storage plus a base index; dereference reads/writes use that element, and `p[i]` from an array-element pointer indexes relative to the base element with the existing array-pointer out-of-bounds diagnostic shape.
- 2026-05-05: No external documentation was needed for deterministic lexer/parser safety tests or the `cust --tokens` CLI flag. The safety tests use fixed local generators plus `std::panic::catch_unwind` to assert generated malformed source and arbitrary bytes decoded with `String::from_utf8_lossy` return either values or `CustError`s rather than panicking; `--tokens` reuses Cust's lexer and prints line/column/debug token records without interpreting the source.
- 2026-05-05: No external documentation was needed for `&p[i]` on array-backed pointer parameters or the `cust --ast` CLI flag. Implementation extends the existing interpreter-owned pointer model by reusing checked pointer indexing to create array-element pointer targets from pointer variables, and `--ast` reuses lexing/parsing only, producing deterministic function-order debug output without interpreting runtime behavior.
- 2026-05-05: No external documentation was needed for the `cust --max-steps N` CLI flag. Implementation defines the flag as a total interpreted loop-iteration budget for `while` and `for` bodies, leaving `cust::interpret` defaults unchanged while exposing `cust::interpret_with_options` for the CLI path.
- 2026-05-05: Pointer diagnostics/conformance coverage now includes invalid fixtures for negative pointer indices, pointer-vs-nonzero-integer equality, unsupported pointer arithmetic, and unsupported pointer ordering. `src/lib.rs` reports explicit diagnostics for the unsupported pointer/integer cases while preserving valid `p == 0`, `0 != p`, pointer truthiness, and pointer equality semantics.
- 2026-05-05: No external documentation was needed for missing-assignment parser diagnostics. Implementation adds a contextual `expect_assign_after()` helper in `src/lib.rs` and routes likely malformed assignment/declaration statements through assignment parsing so exact diagnostics report `expected '=' after ...` for variable declarations, pointer declarations, scalar assignments, indexed assignments, and dereference assignments while preserving ordinary expression statements.
- 2026-05-05: No external documentation was needed for missing-name/type parser diagnostics. Implementation adds contextual `expect_ident_after()` / `expect_type_after()` parser helpers in `src/lib.rs` so malformed function headers, variable/pointer declarations, and parameter lists report exact function/variable/pointer/parameter name or parameter type contexts instead of generic `expected identifier` / `expected type` messages.
- 2026-05-05: No external documentation was needed for parser EOF/array-length edge diagnostics. Implementation reuses existing `parse_block_after(context)` data so EOF inside a block reports the block-introducing context, and extends `expect_array_len()` so empty `[]` and negative `[-1]` array lengths report targeted errors in declarations and fixed-length array parameter spellings. As of the 2026-05-08 unsized-array-parameter run, empty `[]` is accepted in parameter lists as C-style pointer syntax.
- 2026-05-05: During Docker verification, `tests/cli.rs` exposed a parallel-test temp-file collision: multiple tests in the same process could generate the same `/tmp/cust-cli-<pid>-<nanos>.c` path, causing wrong AST output or failed cleanup. Fixed by adding an atomic per-process suffix to the temp source helper; no external documentation was needed.
- 2026-05-05: No external documentation was needed for v0.1 release notes/tag preparation. The release notes were derived from the verified local roadmap/status, fixture coverage, CLI tests, and Docker gate; the only unresolved release-readiness blocker remains the project-owner license choice for a root `LICENSE` file.
- 2026-05-05: No external documentation was needed for pointer-specific parser diagnostics. Implementation keeps unsupported pointer forms fail-fast in `src/lib.rs`: `int *f(...)` now reports unsupported pointer return types before function-name parsing, and `int *values[2]` / `int f(int *values[2])` report unsupported pointer array declarations/parameters at the `[` token while pointer-parameter list boundary tests document existing comma/name diagnostics.
- 2026-05-05: No external documentation was needed for the parser-diagnostics continuation. Implementation remains local to `src/lib.rs`: unsupported `int **` parameter/declaration forms are rejected at the second `*`, and parameter/call argument parsing now treats delimiters after a comma (`)`, `;`, `{`, `}`, EOF) as missing-item diagnostics instead of descending into generic type/expression parsing.
- 2026-05-05: No external documentation was needed for assignment expressions. Implementation follows Cust's existing expression/parser model by adding right-associative assignment at the lowest precedence for supported lvalues (`name`, `array[index]`, `*pointer`) while keeping index expressions delimiter-oriented so malformed `array[0 = ...` still reports the established missing-`]` diagnostic.
- 2026-05-05: No external documentation was needed for the conditional operator implementation. Cust now parses `?:` between logical-or and assignment precedence, parses the middle operand as a full expression, parses the else operand right-associatively, evaluates only the selected branch, and reuses existing truthiness/pointer-evaluation paths in `src/lib.rs`.
- 2026-05-05: No external documentation was needed for the do-while implementation. Cust now lexes `do`, parses `do { ... } while (expr);`, executes the body before checking the condition, consumes loop-step budgets for do-while iterations, and treats `continue` as jumping to the post-body condition check while `break` exits the loop.
- 2026-05-05: No external documentation was needed for compound assignment. Implementation reuses Cust's existing assignment-expression/lvalue model by lexing `+=`/`-=`, parsing them right-associatively at assignment precedence for scalar, array-index, pointer-index, and dereferenced-pointer lvalues, and evaluating them as read-current/apply/store operations that return the assigned scalar value while preserving unsupported pointer-arithmetic diagnostics.
- 2026-05-05: No external documentation was needed for increment/decrement. Implementation reuses Cust's lvalue model by lexing `++`/`--`, parsing prefix operators at unary precedence and postfix operators immediately after primary/grouped expressions, accepting scalar variables, indexed array/pointer elements, and dereferenced pointers as modifiable lvalues, and evaluating prefix forms to the updated value while postfix forms return the previous value. Unsupported pointer-variable increment/decrement preserves the explicit `pointer arithmetic is not supported` diagnostic.
- 2026-05-05: No external documentation was needed for bitwise and shift operators. Implementation follows the established recursive-descent precedence model in `src/lib.rs`: shifts parse between additive and comparison, bitwise `&`, `^`, and `|` parse between equality and logical-and, unary `~` evaluates with Rust/C two's-complement-style integer bitwise complement over Cust's `i64` storage, and pointer operands report an explicit unsupported bitwise diagnostic. During C compiler-oracle verification, `-Werror=parentheses` required the compat fixture to parenthesize intentionally ambiguous precedence expressions while the interpreter-only fixture preserves precedence coverage.
- 2026-05-05: No external documentation was needed for bitwise/shift compound assignments. Implementation extends the existing assignment-expression/lvalue model in `src/lib.rs` by lexing `&=`, `|=`, `^=`, `<<=`, and `>>=`, parsing them at right-associative assignment precedence, reusing checked shift-count diagnostics for shift-assignment RHS values, and preserving explicit pointer-bitwise diagnostics for pointer-valued compound targets.
- 2026-05-05: No external documentation was needed for the comma operator. Implementation extends the existing expression-precedence parser in `src/lib.rs` with comma as the lowest-precedence expression, evaluates the left operand for side effects before yielding the right operand, preserves top-level commas as function-call argument separators by parsing call arguments at assignment precedence, and supports pointer/truthiness contexts by evaluating the right operand through the existing pointer/truthiness paths.
- 2026-05-05: No external documentation was needed for switch statements. Implementation follows the existing block/control-flow architecture in `src/lib.rs` by lexing `switch`/`case`/`default`, parsing integer/character case labels plus default sections, executing from the first matching case/default through later sections for C-style fallthrough, consuming `break` at the switch boundary, and propagating `continue` to enclosing loops. During compiler-oracle verification, GCC/cc with `-Werror` required a `// fall through` annotation in the compat fixture for intentional case fallthrough.
- 2026-05-05: No external documentation was needed for single-statement control bodies. Implementation keeps explicit braced blocks as scoped `Stmt::Block` bodies and parses single unbraced bodies as a single statement after `if`/`else`/`while`/`do`/`for`, preserving C-style `else if` chains, nearest-`if` dangling-`else` binding, loop-control propagation, and no implicit extra block scope for unbraced bodies. During compiler-oracle verification, GCC/cc with `-Werror=dangling-else` required the compat fixture to avoid an intentionally ambiguous dangling-else example; the dangling-else behavior remains covered by the interpreter-only fixture.
- 2026-05-05: No external documentation was needed for duplicate `switch` label diagnostics. Implementation is local parser validation in `src/lib.rs`: each parsed switch tracks seen integer `case` constants and whether `default` already appeared, then reports duplicates at the duplicate case constant/default token before interpretation.
- 2026-05-05: No external documentation was needed for C-style block comments. Implementation is local lexer handling in `src/lib.rs`: `/* ... */` is consumed as whitespace across lines and token boundaries, while EOF before the closing marker reports `unterminated block comment` with the existing source-line/caret helper at the opening delimiter.
- 2026-05-05: No external documentation was needed for `void` helper functions and empty `return;`. Implementation extends Cust's existing function model in `src/lib.rs` with `ReturnType::Int`/`Void`, treats void calls as side-effect-only expression statements via `eval_discard`, and reports explicit diagnostics when void calls are used as scalar expressions or int/void functions return the wrong shape.
- 2026-05-05: No external documentation was needed for global-variable support. Implementation reuses Cust's existing declaration/execution paths by parsing top-level `int`/`char` declarations into `Program::globals`, executing them in a persistent outer interpreter scope before `main()`, and then letting normal nearest-scope lookup/mutation make globals visible to helper functions while preserving local shadowing.
- 2026-05-05: No external documentation was needed for `sizeof` support. Cust deliberately defines interpreter-owned sizes (`sizeof(int) == 8`, `sizeof(char) == 1`, pointer size `8`) instead of depending on host C ABI sizes; native compiler-oracle coverage is limited to stable char/string/char-array size relationships while interpreter fixtures cover Cust-specific int/pointer sizes and non-evaluation semantics.
- 2026-05-06: No external documentation was needed for uninitialized declarations. Cust now defines deterministic interpreter defaults for declarations without initializers (`int`/`char` scalars initialize to `0`, supported pointers initialize to null, arrays continue to zero-initialize); native compiler-oracle coverage is limited to global zero-initialization because C local uninitialized reads are indeterminate.
- 2026-05-06: No external documentation was needed for enum constant declarations. Cust now lexes `enum`, parses optional enum tags plus enumerators with explicit integer values, implicit incrementing values, and trailing commas, and stores enum constants as scoped read-only integer identifiers; native compiler-oracle coverage verifies the supported subset against C.
- 2026-05-06: No external documentation was needed for function prototypes. Implementation remains parser-local: top-level function headers ending in `;` are stored as signatures, ignored at runtime, and checked for compatibility with later definitions or prior declarations; native compiler-oracle coverage verifies `int`, `void`, pointer, and string-decay prototype signatures in the supported subset.
- 2026-05-06: No external documentation was needed for `char` function returns. Cust's existing scalar storage model already represents `char` values as integers, so implementation generalized function return metadata from `int`/`void` to scalar `CType`/`void`, allowing `char name(...)` definitions/prototypes and `sizeof(char_call())` to use the declared return type while preserving `void` scalar-use diagnostics.
- 2026-05-06: No external documentation was needed for the scoped pointer-arithmetic milestone. Native compiler-oracle coverage is limited to ABI-stable `char` array compound literal sizes, while interpreter coverage includes Cust-specific `int` and aggregate sizes.

Cust follows the existing scalar compound-literal initializer parser but now routes cast/compound-literal expressions through postfix parsing so `(int){9}++` binds correctly, evaluates the scalar initializer before assignment/compound/increment to preserve side effects, and returns C-compatible scalar assignment/increment results without requiring hidden persistent storage unless the literal's address is taken.
- 2026-05-09: No external documentation was needed for aggregate compound-literal scalar-field lvalues. Implementation reuses `AggregateFieldGet` parser lowering for dotted scalar fields on aggregate compound literals, adds scalar assignment/compound/increment expression variants that evaluate the aggregate initializer first, returns the assigned/updated or previous scalar result according to C lvalue operator rules, and rejects writes to const fields with the existing `cannot assign to const struct field '<field>'` diagnostic.
- 2026-05-09: No external documentation was needed for aggregate compound-literal pointer-field reads. Implementation reuses the existing aggregate compound-literal field lowering, adds metadata inference for `AggregateFieldGet` pointer contexts, and evaluates pointer-valued fields from the temporary aggregate field map so `((struct Cursor){values + 1}).p` participates in pointer declarations, calls, arithmetic, dereference, and equality without host addresses.
- 2026-05-09: No external documentation was needed for aggregate compound-literal array-field decay. Implementation reuses the hidden current-scope compound-literal storage pattern for embedded aggregate-array fields by deep-cloning the field elements into a hidden `Value::StructArray`, while scalar array fields continue to use shared `ArrayValue` storage; const-discard metadata now uses field `is_const` for array fields and `points_to_const` for pointer fields.
- 2026-05-09: No external documentation was needed for aggregate compound-literal array-field indexing/address-of. Parser postfix indexing lowers `AggregateFieldGet[index]` through existing pointer decay/arithmetic/dereference machinery, covering scalar array-field indexing, aggregate-array field element field access, and `&...field[index]` pointer identity while preserving const-discard diagnostics. The run also fixed a pointer-arithmetic classification pitfall where literal `0` could be probed as a null pointer in `pointer + 0`; literal integer offsets are now handled before pointer probing.
- 2026-05-09: No external documentation was needed for field access on aggregate-valued expression results. Parser `.` routing now wraps aggregate-capable grouped expressions (`Assign`, aggregate `DerefSet`, `Conditional`, `Comma`, and aggregate-returning `Call`) in the existing `AggregateFieldGet` path, while runtime evaluation reuses `eval_struct_expr` so by-value assignment, conditional/comma, pointer-dereference assignment, and function-return semantics stay centralized.
- 2026-05-09: No external documentation was needed for field access on union-valued expression results. - 2026-05-09: No external documentation was needed for scalar-context diagnostic polish. Implementation is local to scalar expression evaluation in `src/lib.rs`: pointer-returning calls now report function-specific scalar-use diagnostics, and aggregate compound literals use the resolved aggregate kind so union values no longer fall through to struct wording.
- 2026-05-09: No external documentation was needed for `sizeof` on fields selected from aggregate-valued expressions. Implementation stays in non-evaluating metadata inference in `src/lib.rs`: `sizeof_expr` now infers the aggregate root type for assignment, conditional, comma, function-call, dereference-assignment, and union-valued expression forms before applying existing deterministic field sizing; compiler-oracle coverage uses `sizeof(field) == sizeof(type)` instead of native exact byte sizes.
- 2026-05-09: No external documentation was needed for full array-object `sizeof` on array fields selected from aggregate-valued expressions. The fix is metadata-only in `sizeof_aggregate_field_type`: final `StructFieldType::StructArray` fields now multiply the deterministic embedded aggregate element size by the field length, matching the existing scalar-array field path and preserving non-evaluation of calls/assignments/conditionals inside the `sizeof` operand. Native compiler-oracle coverage uses array-length ratios instead of ABI-dependent byte totals.
- 2026-05-09: No external documentation was needed for const pointer-returning call conversion. The root cause was metadata-only: `pointer_expr_points_to_const` already handled variables, arrays, fields, assignments, conditionals, and comma expressions, but not `Expr::Call`, so direct conversion checks could miss a function's declared `const T *` return while the runtime pointer value still flowed. It now reads `ReturnType::Pointer { points_to_const, .. }` without evaluating the call before conversion checks.
- 2026-05-09: No external documentation was needed for C reverse-subscript expressions. C defines `a[b]` as `*(a + b)`, so Cust now lowers otherwise-unhandled postfix subscript targets through the existing safe pointer arithmetic and dereference path. This covers integer-left forms such as `1[p]`, `1["hi"]`, `1[(int[]){...}]`, and aggregate pointer field access like `1[points].y` without introducing host addresses or new runtime pointer machinery.

Implementation is parser-local: direct `&"text"[i]` lowers to the same string-literal array-base pointer plus offset used by grouped `&("text")[i]` and reverse `&i["text"]` forms, preserving read-only string storage diagnostics through the existing pointer machinery.

Implementation is parser-local: after consuming a `Token::StringLiteral`, Cust greedily consumes immediately adjacent string-literal tokens, removes the intermediate NUL terminator, and extends the byte vector so existing string pointer, index, `sizeof`, char-array initializer, and char-array compound literal paths see one NUL-terminated string value.

Implementation deliberately reuses Cust's existing interpreter-owned pointer-difference metadata so relational comparisons are only defined for pointers into the same supported scalar/string/aggregate array storage; different-array comparisons get a compare-specific diagnostic while scalar/null pointer ordering stays unsupported.

- 2026-05-10: No external documentation was needed for multidimensional array diagnostics. Cust intentionally keeps the current supported array subset one-dimensional; parser checks now report targeted unsupported diagnostics immediately after the first parsed `[...]` when a second `[` follows in declarations, function parameters, or aggregate fields instead of falling through to generic semicolon or parameter-list boundary errors.

Implementation is lexer-local: after parsing the existing decimal/octal/hex digit span into Cust's deterministic `i64` value, `consume_integer_suffix` accepts standard unsigned/long suffix spellings (`u`, `U`, `l`, `L`, `ll`, `LL`, and combinations such as `UL`, `lu`, `uL`, `LLU`) without changing runtime numeric storage or parser tokens.

- 2026-05-10: No external documentation was needed for signed/unsigned char type spellings. Implementation is parser-level syntax parity: `signed char` and `unsigned char` now lower to Cust's existing deterministic `char` storage in declarations, functions, pointer contexts, typedefs, casts, and `sizeof`; runtime char storage and `sizeof(char) == 1` remain unchanged rather than adopting native signedness/range or wraparound semantics.
- 2026-05-10: No external documentation was needed for long/short type spellings. - 2026-05-11: No external documentation was needed for local `auto`/`register` storage-class specifier parsing. Implementation keeps both specifiers as parser-level syntax over Cust's existing automatic local storage paths for block-scope and `for` initializer declarations; native compiler-oracle coverage avoids taking the address of `register` variables so `-std=c11 -Wall -Wextra -Werror` remains warning-free.
- 2026-05-11: No external documentation was needed for one-dimensional array typedef aliases. - 2026-05-11: No external documentation was needed for top-level `extern` function storage-class syntax. Cust treats `extern` on top-level function prototypes/definitions as parser-only single-file linkage metadata, reusing existing function/prototype signature compatibility; C compiler-oracle fixtures avoid initialized `extern` globals because native `-Wall -Wextra -Werror` may treat them as warning-prone.
- 2026-05-11: No external documentation was needed for uninitialized top-level `extern` object declarations. Cust treats supported `extern` globals without initializers as parser-only declarations in its single-file interpreter model, so a later ordinary scalar/array/aggregate/enum/pointer definition owns the runtime storage and duplicate-global diagnostics are avoided. Compiler-oracle fixtures keep aggregate definitions before extern object declarations and still avoid initialized `extern` globals under `-Wall -Wextra -Werror`.

Implementation treats `enum { ... } value` and `enum Tag { ... } value` as scalar integer declarations but preserves the parsed enum constants by wrapping the resulting declaration in a preceding runtime `EnumDecl` statement, so same-statement initializers such as `enum { READY = 1 } state = READY;` can resolve the constants before variable initialization. - 2026-06-25: No external documentation was needed for inline named aggregate definitions in function return type specifiers. Root cause was parser routing: function lookahead skipped only `struct Tag`, not `struct Tag { ... }`, so top-level `struct Pair { int x; int y; } make_pair(int);` was parsed as an aggregate variable declaration and failed at the function declarator `(`. The fix adds aggregate-definition-aware function lookahead and routes return-type `struct`/`union` definitions through shared aggregate-definition parsing after the keyword has been consumed. Native compiler-oracle fixtures avoid top-level `const` aggregate returns because `cc -std=c11 -Wall -Wextra -Werror` reports `-Werror=ignored-qualifiers`; const return coverage remains interpreter-only. - 2026-06-26: No external documentation was needed for inline enum definitions in cast type specifiers. The fix reuses the existing pending-inline-enum wrapper for expression and return statements; declaration lists were already covered by variable-declaration wrapping.

- 2026-06-26: No external documentation was needed for inline named aggregate definitions in expression type contexts. - 2026-06-30: No external documentation was needed for inline aggregate definitions inside pointer arithmetic and pointer-index expressions. - 2026-06-30: Native `cc -std=c11 -Wall -Wextra -Werror` rejects leading non-pointer `restrict` qualifiers such as `restrict int value` with `invalid use of 'restrict'`, while Cust's supported `restrict` subset is explicitly parser-level metadata on post-star pointer declarators (`int * restrict p`, parameters, and aggregate fields). - 2026-07-03: No external documentation was needed for declaration initializer missing-expression diagnostics. The root cause was parser routing: declaration parsers consumed `=` and then delegated delimiter-leading inputs such as `int value = ;`, `int *slot = ,`, and `struct Point point = ;` to the generic expression parser, producing `expected expression, found ...`. The root cause was parser-local: array, struct/union, scalar braced, and struct-array initializer parsers detected over-capacity entries before parsing the excess token but created unlocated `CustError::new(...)` diagnostics. These routes now capture `peek_located()` before returning the `too many initializers for ...` error so diagnostics point at the first excess initializer token while preserving existing missing-element and trailing-comma behavior.
- 2026-07-03: No external documentation was needed for excess compound-literal initializer source locations. The root cause matched ordinary braced initializers: fixed-size scalar array compound literal and aggregate-array compound literal parsers detected over-capacity positional entries before parsing the excess token but returned unlocated `CustError::new(...)` diagnostics. These routes now capture `peek_located()` and use `Parser::error_at(...)`, preserving existing fixed-size length enforcement while pointing at the first excess initializer token.
- 2026-07-03: No external documentation was needed for malformed path-designator diagnostics. - 2026-07-05: No external documentation was needed for delimiter-only array-length diagnostics. - 2026-07-11: Native `cc -std=c11 -Wall -Wextra -Werror` accepts legal one-level post-star-qualified pointer type names (`sizeof(int * const)`, `_Alignof(int * volatile)`, `(int * const)0`) and also accepts nested pointer type spellings. Cust intentionally supports only one pointer level, so its cast and `sizeof`/`_Alignof` parsers now consume valid post-star qualifiers before checking for a second `*`; that second star produces the existing targeted `pointer-to-pointer ... not supported` boundary diagnostic rather than a generic closing-delimiter error. Root cause: after `parse_cast()` consumed a supported pointer star in scalar, named/anonymous aggregate, or pointer-typedef type names, it immediately required `)`, so `(int *[2])0` reported a generic closing-delimiter error. Cust already rejects pointer arrays in declaration, typedef, `sizeof`, and `_Alignof` type routes; cast routing now checks an immediate `[` after the pointer type and reports `pointer array casts are not supported` at that token. These array cast forms are intentionally unsupported by Cust's safe one-level-pointer subset.
- 2026-07-11: No external documentation was needed for abstract function type-name diagnostics. Root cause: after consuming a supported scalar or aggregate type in `(int(void))0`, `sizeof(int(void))`, or `_Alignof(int(void))`, parser routes delegated the function declarator suffix to generic closing-parenthesis helpers. `reject_function_type_suffix()` now detects `(` not immediately followed by `*` after supported cast/type-query type names and reports a source-located unsupported-function-type diagnostic, deliberately preserving the existing parenthesized-pointer cast/type-query diagnostics.
